# Change Log

Newest first, one entry per completed task, each with the commit it landed in. The earlier
multi-service version of the project (2024–2025) was removed in `011537c` ("from scratch").

## 2026-10-05 · Deploy scripts: defect 5, the Ubuntu 26.04 test failure, D-50, checklist rules, OPS-2b's checklist (OPS-2b)

Commits on `feature/family-budget`: `2d4797b` (the deploy facts, the entry below), `aaf8391` ("ops: the image store
and platform named (defect 5)"), then "test: no shared ECB rate or JVM default left behind", which holds this entry as
it stands. Not pushed, not merged, not deployed.

**Changes**
- Defect 5: `run`'s step 1.2, `verify` and `switch`'s step 1.4 printed "Image store: unknown, platform unknown" in
  production (2026-10-04), while `docker info` and `docker version` answered there as the pre-flight showed.
  - The cause, in the scripts, not in Docker's answers: `image_store` and `server_platform` set `IMAGE_STORE` and
    `PLATFORM`, but OPS-2's scripts called them only through `content_of_container`, `content_of_image` and
    `content_of_id`, each inside `$(…)`. A subshell's variables end with it, so the shell that printed the line never
    had them, on any store. Each subshell ran `docker info` and `docker version` again for itself and read every content
    identity correctly, so no verdict was wrong (production's `verify` named both images "the recorded image").
  - Reproduced with real Docker, by sourcing `deploy/common.sh` in a subshell and calling its read-only functions as
    `deploy.sh` does: on the laptop's daemon (Docker 29.8.1, classic store, overlay2) the content of a running
    container came back and the line said "unknown, platform unknown"; in a throwaway `docker:29-dind` (29.8.2, the
    containerd store; its `docker info -f '{{json .DriverStatus}}'` printed byte for byte production's
    `[["driver-type","io.containerd.snapshotter.v1"]]`, its `docker version` `linux/amd64`), with two containers
    named as production's, run from an attested build (ID `sha256:3989f20f…`, a manifest list; content
    `sha256:29bcaed0…`), the same. Called in the shell itself, the functions answered "classic, linux/amd64" and
    "containerd, linux/amd64".
  - The stub: its containerd answer and its `docker version` answer were already production's, byte for byte, so it
    didn't differ where it mattered; it couldn't show the defect, which is a shell variable lost in a subshell. Its
    classic answer was shorter than real Docker's and is now the laptop's 29.8.1 answer byte for byte; "broken" now
    answers as the 29.x client without a daemon (`docker info` prints `null` and the error, exit 1; `docker version`
    an empty line and the error); a new store "unrecognised" answers `null` with exit 0. The 85 cases missed the
    defect because none looked at the line: they checked the content identities and the verdicts, which were right.
    `identity_unavailable` even asserted "(image store unknown", which held for the wrong reason: the line said
    unknown on every store.
  - `deploy/common.sh`: `docker_identity` reads both in the calling shell and sets `STORE_LINE`; `running_images`
    calls it first, in the script's own shell, so every subshell after it inherits both, and `docker info` runs once
    per script instead of once per subshell. When Docker answers something the scripts can't parse, or fails, the
    line names the command and its answer or error (`docker_error`, `one_line`), never a bare "unknown":
    `Image store: not known (docker info -f '{{json .DriverStatus}}' answered null), platform linux/amd64`.
  - `deploy/deploy.sh`: step 1.2, its refusal when a content can't be read, `verify` and `switch`'s step 1.4 print
    `STORE_LINE`. `deploy/rollback.sh`: step 1 prints it.
  - Tags: the scripts and the stub both name commit tags by the full hash (`finance-tracker-api:<40 hex>`), as
    production holds them. What named them by 7 characters was text: the summary's "Images" line (`KEPT_LINE`, "kept
    as :4510003 and :previous") and step 3.2's "take no tag" line, which is how OPS-2's checklist came to inspect
    `:4510003`, an image that doesn't exist. Both now name the full tag.
- What `b6870f2`'s scripts, which run in production now, do there (production's state after OPS-2's deploy and the
  acceptance checks: `last-good` naming `b6870f2` with api `bb3bdef3…`, gone from the store, and its content
  `9315f0d9…`; `contents` holding both pairs, recorded by OPS-2's `finish`; `:previous` and `:4510003…` api
  `6d6f35b8…` and web `bba7ff04…`; `:latest` `41e46a73…` and `827620f3…`, the same contents):
  - OPS-2b's `run`, which is `b6870f2`'s (bash reads it before the merge): step 1.2 prints defect 5's line, reads both
    contents from the containers' `ImageManifestDescriptor`, finds both the recorded images by ID and content, and goes
    on. Step 3.2 looks for the api's content in the recorded ID (gone), the running ID (the same, gone),
    `:b6870f2…` (not there yet) and `:previous`, which holds it: it tags `finance-tracker-api:previous` as
    `:b6870f2b6272…` and `:previous` (unchanged, `6d6f35b8…`), the web's own `bba7ff04…` likewise, and writes
    `b6870f2…` into the previous-commit file; then it removes the tags of the oldest of the four commits,
    `8ede02e…`, keeping `b6870f2…`, `4510003…` and `f0425c0…`. No wrong tag and no failure: **no remedy is needed**.
    Step 1.8 preserves the before-dump (D-43's first run in production).
  - `switch off`: reaches its confirmation, as on 2026-10-04 (H). `switch on`: REFUSED at 1.5, already on.
  - `rollback.sh 4510003`: the target `4510003`, the commit OPS-2's run replaced; its api by content (`:4510003…`
    is `6d6f35b8…`, the recorded `bb3bdef3…` known as `9315f0d9…` from `contents`), its web by ID.
  - `verify`: OK, with defect 5's line.
  - After OPS-2b's deploy, OPS-2b's `finish`, `verify` (now "Image store: containerd, platform linux/amd64"), `switch`
    and `rollback.sh b6870f2` (its api by content, `INFO`, its web by ID) work on what `b6870f2`'s `run` wrote, and a
    run of OPS-2b's commit again is refused (D-42).
- Tests (`deploy/tests/run.sh`, 89 cases):
  - `store_and_platform_named`: the line in `run`, `verify`, `switch` and `rollback.sh` on the classic and the
    containerd store, `docker info` read at most once per script, an answer it can't parse and a platform it can't
    read named with what Docker said. `identity_unavailable` expects the daemon's error in the line and never a bare
    "unknown". `happy_path` and `different_content` expect the full tag in the summary and in step 3.2.
  - `production_2026_10_05`, a model of production now with the real IDs and contents of the pre-flight and the run
    where they were given in full, and three cross-version cases that run `b6870f2`'s scripts, taken from git:
    `b6870f2_scripts_on_production_now` (its `verify`, `switch off` answered no, `switch on` and `rollback.sh` answered
    no, nothing changed), `b6870f2_run_deploys_ops2b` (an api of new content: recreated, D-25 logged anew) and
    `b6870f2_run_deploys_ops2b_same_api` (nothing recreated, D-25 from 2026-10-03), each followed by OPS-2b's
    `finish`, `verify`, a run again, `rollback.sh` and `switch off` and `on`.
  - `deploy/tests/mutate.sh`, 50 mutations: three new ones (the store read only in subshells; a bare unknown for
    `docker info`'s answer; the kept tag named by 7 characters).

- The `RateApiTests` failure on Ubuntu 26.04 ("CI on Ubuntu 26.04" #1 on `b6870f2`: `aManualRateIsTheUsersOwn`,
  expected "2026-09-01" but was "2026-09-25"): the root cause is in the tests only.
  - `LedgerRepositoryTests.exchangeRateIsInsertedThenReplaced` saves an ECB rate, EUR→RUB 75.50 on 2026-09-25 with
    `user_id` NULL, in the database every integration test shares, and never removes it. ECB rates are every user's,
    so any class after it reads that rate, until `EcbRateLoaderTests` deletes every ECB rate. `RateApiTests` saves
    Alice's manual RUB rate of 2026-09-01 and expects it to be her latest; with the stray rate, GET `/api/rates`
    rightly answers the later ECB one (2026-09-25, `ECB`, `inLedger` false), as the run's log showed.
  - So it depends only on the class order: `LedgerRepositoryTests` before `RateApiTests` with `EcbRateLoaderTests`
    not between them. Surefire's default order follows the file system's listing, which differs between runners;
    Ubuntu 24.04's happened to be safe.
  - Reproduced locally at `b6870f2` with exactly those two classes in alphabetical order (`ledger.LedgerRepositoryTests`
    sorts before `ledger.api.RateApiTests`): the same assertion and values. The same failure with the JVM started in
    `Pacific/Kiritimati`, `Etc/GMT+12` and `UTC`: the time zone plays no part. In reverse alphabetical order the two
    pass: `RateApiTests` runs first. The whole suite at `b6870f2` passes in both alphabetical and reverse alphabetical order (404 tests each): in
    alphabetical order `FamilyCurrencyApiTests` runs between the two, and its `@AfterEach` deletes the ECB rates of
    August and September 2026, the stray one included (`EcbRateLoaderTests` deletes them all). The failure needs
    neither between them, as the 26.04 runner's order had it.
  - The +14:00 in the log from one class on: `IntegrationTest`'s static block called
    `TimeZone.setDefault(Pacific/Kiritimati)` when the first class that touches it loaded (an integration test, or
    `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests` and `LedgerSchemaTests` through `IntegrationTest.POSTGRES`), and never set it back;
    the classes before it ran in the runner's zone (UTC). Deliberate in intent (CLAUDE.md: the JVM runs in
    Kiritimati), but JVM-wide and order-dependent. Not the cause of this failure.
  - Production code: the rates (`RateService`, `RateBook`, the ECB loader) read no clock and no default zone. Two
    other places call `LocalDate.now()` (the JVM's zone and the system clock) instead of `Today`: `DemoController`
    (the demo's last day) and `ReportController` (a report's default `asOf`). In production both give `Today`'s date
    (the api's zone is UTC, `Today`'s clock the system's); only a test that sets `TestClock` sees them differ. Not a
    cause here, and unchanged: an open question below.
- The fix, in the tests:
  - `LedgerRepositoryTests` removes the ECB rates it wrote after each test (`@AfterEach`).
  - `IntegrationTest` fails a class that leaves any ECB rate (`user_id` NULL) in the shared database (`@AfterAll`,
    plain JDBC), naming the rows.
  - `IntegrationTest` no longer sets the time zone. `pom.xml`: the property `user.timezone`, `Pacific/Kiritimati`, and
    surefire's `argLine` `-Duser.timezone=${user.timezone}`, so the test JVM runs in Kiritimati from its start; on
    Maven's command line `-Duser.timezone=…` overrides it (checked with `help:effective-pom`).
  - `JvmDefaultsGuard` (a JUnit extension for every class: `src/test/resources/junit-platform.properties` turns on
    autodetection, `META-INF/services/org.junit.jupiter.api.extension.Extension` lists it) fails a class that leaves
    the JVM's default time zone or locale (default, display, format) changed, and puts them back so the classes after
    it run as before. `JvmDefaultsGuardTests`: unchanged passes; a changed zone or locale fails naming the class, and
    is put back.
  - Whether this reaches the api image: yes. The api's build context is `backend/` and its Dockerfile copies `pom.xml`
    and `src`, tests included; Maven then writes a new jar (no `project.build.outputTimestamp`). In a throwaway
    `docker:29-dind` (29.8.2, the containerd store): `b6870f2`'s `backend/` built twice gave the same content
    (`sha256:8c914ee1…`); the same with one comment added to a test file gave other content (`sha256:c97a6ff7…`);
    OPS-2b's `backend/` gave other content too (`sha256:00350289…`). So OPS-2b's `run` recreates api (OPS-2b's checklist).

**Verified**
- Commit 3: `./mvnw -B clean verify` five ways, each 407 tests (404 and `JvmDefaultsGuardTests`' 3), 0 failures, 0
  errors, 0 skipped, BUILD SUCCESS: the default order; `-Dsurefire.runOrder=reversealphabetical`;
  `-Duser.timezone=Pacific/Kiritimati`; `-Duser.timezone=Etc/GMT+12` (every Spring Boot line of its log at -12:00);
  and `-Dsurefire.runOrder=alphabetical`. The default run's log has every line at +14:00 from the first class on, and
  no guard message. Before the fix, `LedgerRepositoryTests` then `RateApiTests` failed as on Ubuntu 26.04; after it
  they pass in that order. The guard, before `IntegrationTest`'s static block was removed, in a JVM started in UTC:
  it failed `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests` ("left the JVM's defaults changed: time zone UTC … before it, time zone
  Pacific/Kiritimati … after it"). The shared-rate check, with `LedgerRepositoryTests`' cleanup disabled on a copy:
  it failed that class with `2026-09-25 RUB ECB`; the file was put back.
- Commit 2: `deploy/tests/run.sh` 89 of 89; `deploy/tests/mutate.sh` all 50 caught; ShellCheck 0.11.0 (`-x`, CI's
  file list) clean. The real-Docker reproduction above, before and after the fix, the after on three daemons: the
  laptop's ("Image store: classic, platform linux/amd64"), the dind ("containerd, linux/amd64", both contents) and an
  address without a daemon (the client's error in both halves of the line).

**Open**
- For the PM (D-49, F8): `DemoController` and `ReportController`'s default `asOf` take `LocalDate.now()`, the JVM's
  zone and the system clock, where the rest of the app takes `Today`. They agree in production; a test that sets the
  clock can't move them. Options: leave them; or have both take `Today`, which a test then controls. Unchanged here.
- OPS-2b isn't pushed, merged or deployed: defect 5 stays in production until then.

## 2026-10-04 · Deploy scripts: OPS-2's defects 1 to 4, CI before 2026-10-19, OPS-2's checklist (OPS-2)

Commits on `feature/family-budget`: `c45d956` (the deploy facts, the entry below), `685deb3` ("ops: the before-dump
preserved (D-43)"), `789b8c6` ("ops: images by content identity (defect 4)"), `f1c2f5c` ("ops: a refused switch
blocks nothing (defect 1)"), `bd77f05` ("ops: no run over the running commit after its finish (D-42)"), `83abd3e`
("ci: ubuntu-24.04, Node 24 actions, a manual Ubuntu 26.04 run (D-44)"), then "docs: OPS-2's deploy analysis and
checklist", whose hash OPS-2's report names (the commit to deploy).
Not pushed, not merged, not deployed.

**Changes**
- Defect 3 (D-43, decided by the PM): the before-dump could be overwritten. pg-backup names dumps by the UTC
  minute, and on 2026-10-03 the after-dump of 09:23:38Z replaced the before-dump of 09:23:18Z.
  - `deploy/deploy.sh`, step 1.8: after the restore test, `preserve_before_dump` copies the dump into the run folder
    (`cp`, mode 600; a hard link would change with an in-place rewrite) and appends `before_dump`,
    `before_dump_size`, `before_dump_sha256` and `before_dump_copy` to the run's `meta`. `backup` now reads each
    dump's SHA-256 and checks that the file `last-success` names exists.
  - Step 4.6: `wait_for_another_minute` waits, saying so, while a dump written now would get the before-dump's name;
    `check_dumps` then requires another name and the copy's recorded SHA-256. Either failure is a FAILED after the
    merge that names the preserved copy.
  - The summary's "Before:" and "After:" lines carry each dump's name, size, SHA-256 and restore test, and "Before:"
    the copy's path. `sha256sum` joins the tools step 1.1 checks.
  - `deploy/rollback.sh`: the refusal below V7 (D-22) names the copy that HEAD's deploy run preserved, with its
    SHA-256, or says that run predates D-43.
  - The runbook: steps 1.8 and 4.6, the run folder's files, and "The preserved dumps", how to prune them by hand.
    D-43 in `docs/family-budget/requirements.md`, next to D-41.
  - Tests: the systemctl stub numbers its dumps and can name them by the minute as pg-backup does
    (`dump-minute-names`) or rewrite the first dump in place (`dump-overwrite`); the pg-restore-test stub runs
    `on-restore-test.<n>` at its n-th call. New cases `dumps_in_the_same_minute` (the wait, two names, the copy as
    recorded, 600, not a hard link, the summary), `dump_overwritten_in_place` and `preserved_dump_changed`;
    `rollback_below_v7` checks the copy named. Five mutations: a hard link, no wait, no name check, no SHA-256 check,
    no copy named by `rollback.sh`.

- Defect 4: images compared by ID, which in the containerd image store says nothing about content.
  - Reproduced on the laptop with temporary images only (a busybox Dockerfile, a compose project
    `ops2-d4-repro`), removed afterwards. The laptop's own Docker 29.8.1 uses the classic store (overlay2): two
    builds gave the same ID, `sha256:d8261eca…`, the config digest, and `up -d` left the container "Running". In a
    throwaway `docker:29-dind` (Docker 29.8.2, `driver-type io.containerd.snapshotter.v1`, Compose 5.5.1), with the
    BuildKit output's "unpacking to": the first build exported manifest `sha256:3f6284ad…`, config
    `sha256:52651eda…`, attestation manifest `sha256:89fb42b6…` and manifest list `sha256:c3602d95…`, the image's ID;
    the second, of the same content, the same manifest and config but attestation `sha256:1210c716…` and list
    `sha256:69d5074a…`, a new ID. `up -d` left the container on `c3602d95…` ("Running"): Compose's label
    `com.docker.compose.image` holds the manifest's digest, `3f6284ad…`, which didn't change. Changing the service's
    environment (as `switch` changes `.env`) recreated it from `:latest`, `69d5074a…`. Meanwhile `c3602d95…` was gone
    from the store ("No such image" for `docker image inspect` and `docker tag`; containerd's image list held only
    the new list), while the container ran it.
  - So in production: every build of the same content gets a new ID (the attestation manifest differs at every build,
    and with it the list's digest); `run`'s `up -d` doesn't recreate a container whose manifest is the same,
    while `switch`'s restart, for another environment, does, from the newer `:latest`; between the two, the tag
    named the newer list while the container ran the older one, which no tag held any more.
  - The content identity, read from the store, not a build change: `docker image inspect --platform <os/arch>`
    answers the platform manifest's digest in the containerd store and the config digest (the ID) in the classic
    store, and a container's `ImageManifestDescriptor` holds that manifest's digest (none in the classic store, where
    its `.Image` is the config digest). That works for images already built and state already written, which a
    build without attestations can't fix, and the ID in the containerd store would still be a manifest's, not the
    config's. Building without attestations would also change the services' build options, so Compose would
    recreate api and web at OPS-2's deploy, and what is now proven to run would be rebuilt.
  - `deploy/common.sh`: `image_store` and `server_platform` (from `docker info` and `docker version`),
    `content_of_image`, `content_of_container`, `content_of_id` (recorded, else from the image, else from a
    container that runs it), `running_images`, `compare_identity` (the same ID; `INFO` for another ID with the same
    content; other content; unknown, never taken as the same). A new state file `contents` (`<image ID> <content>`
    lines, parsed only) keeps an ID's content after the store deleted the image. `last-good` gains `api_content`
    and `web_content`; a `last-good` without them, as `4510003`'s scripts write it, gets them worked out while the
    image or a container holds it. `write_last_good` takes the two contents.
  - `deploy/deploy.sh`: `run`'s step 1.2 prints the store, the platform and each content, compares them with the
    last good deploy's, and refuses (nothing changed) when the running containers' content can't be read. Step 3.2
    keeps the last good deploy's images by content, tagging whichever image still holds it (the recorded ID, the
    running one, its commit's tag, `:previous` or `:latest`); 4510003's step 3.2 called that state "a failed run's"
    on 2026-10-04. `finish`, `adopt`, the baseline and the postflight record the contents. `verify` has "The images"
    (other content or unknown is a problem, another ID with the same content `INFO`; it writes nothing). `switch`'s
    step 1.4 compares by content (`switch on` refuses on other content or unknown, `switch off` warns and goes on);
    a new step 3.6 prints api's and web's ID and content before and after, "unchanged" only for the same content,
    "recreated with the same content" otherwise, and then makes `last-good` name the new IDs so that the next
    comparison by ID matches too; other content or an unknown one after the restart fails `switch on` and is a
    warning for `switch off`. The summary's "Images" line says which.
  - `deploy/rollback.sh`: step 3 accepts the commit's tag by ID or by content (`INFO`), never an unknown content.
  - Tests: the docker stub has the classic store (the default; a build of the same content keeps its ID), the
    containerd store (`store` file: a new ID at every build, content identities in `content/`, an ID no tag names
    gone, the containers' `ImageManifestDescriptor`, Compose recreating by content) and a broken one; and answers
    `docker info`, `docker version` and `image inspect --platform`. New cases: `same_content_new_id` (2026-10-03's
    `switch on`: recreated with the same content, `last-good` updated, then `verify` and the next run match),
    `last_good_id_differs_same_content` (the state before 2026-10-04's run: `INFO`, the images kept by content, and
    a rollback by content), `different_content`, `identity_unavailable` and `old_format_state`.
    `repeat_after_failure` and `run_fails_on_a_page` read the new wording (after a failed run `verify` now also
    names api and web). Six mutations: compare by ID, unknown taken as the same, switch calling unknown
    "unchanged", switch leaving `last-good`, old `last-good` without contents, `rollback.sh` by ID only.
  - The runbook: "Image identity", steps 1.2 and 3.2, `verify`, `switch`, `rollback.sh`, the `contents` file.

- Defect 1: a refused switch blocked the next one. `4510003`'s `switch` took the newest run folder, whatever its
  status, so a `switch` refused at its confirmation (2026-10-03, 09:05:56Z and 09:20:28Z) made the next one stop at
  step 1.4 with "run deploy.sh finish first", and the one refused as already on (2026-10-04, 14:17:41Z) blocks
  `switch off` in production now. Found along the way: from 2026-10-03T09:25:40Z until the empty run of 2026-10-04,
  `4510003`'s step 1.4 would also have refused `switch off` by image ID (`last-good`'s api `6d6f35b8…` against the
  running `bb3bdef3…`, defect 4).
  - `deploy/common.sh`: `latest_unrefused_run` (the newest folder that isn't `refused`) and `unfinished_work` (why a
    run is real unfinished work, with the command that resolves it: a deploy without its finish, `finish`; a deploy
    that stopped or failed its finish, judge it, then roll back, run again, `finish` again or `adopt`; a switch that
    changed `.env` or restarted api and didn't complete, `switch off`; a rollback that didn't complete, by hand).
  - `deploy/deploy.sh switch`: step 1.4 asks those two; a refused run of any kind never counts. `switch on` refuses
    on unfinished work, naming the command; `switch off` never refuses for it, nor for health (1.3), a HEAD that
    isn't the last good deploy or images that aren't its content: each is a WARNING. Step 1.5 says "nothing to
    switch" only when `.env` and the running api both have the value already; otherwise the same switch restarts
    api. A failed switch names `switch off` as the way, back after a failed `switch on`, again after a failed
    `switch off` (`4510003`'s named `switch on` there, which step 1.4 then refused).
  - `finish`'s "nothing to finish" names `verify`.
  - The runbook's "Switch the family budget on or off".
  - Tests: the docker stub's `up-fails`. New cases `switch_refused_at_the_confirmation` (twice, then through),
    `switch_refused_as_already_on` (production's state after J: `switch off`, then `verify`),
    `switch_refused_at_a_precheck` (a page), `switch_interrupted_after_the_change` (`switch on` refused at health,
    then at 1.4 naming `switch off`, which goes on), `switch_off_after_a_failed_restart` (`.env` false, the api still
    true: `switch off` again restarts it) and `switch_after_an_unfinished_run` (`switch on` names `finish`, `switch
    off` warns). Five mutations: a refused run deciding, switch off refusing for unfinished work, an interrupted
    switch taken as done, "nothing to switch" by `.env` alone, `switch on` named after a failed `switch off`.
  - `switch on` in the interrupted state: REFUSED at step 1.3 while the api is unhealthy, else at step 1.4,
    "… did not complete (failed): run deploy/deploy.sh switch off"; once that `switch off` completes, `switch on`
    works again.

- Defect 2 (D-42, decided by the PM): `run` of the running commit went through as "Commits: none" and re-pointed

  - `deploy/common.sh`: `clean_finish` (the last good deploy, with `finish=passed` or adopted, and its newest history

  - `deploy/deploy.sh run`, step 1.3, right after the fast-forward check: the target equal to `HEAD` after a clean

- CI before 2026-10-19 (D-44, decided by the PM).
  - How `4510003`'s gate counts CI (`ci_check`, unchanged since OPS-1): every check run of the commit through
    GitHub's API (`/commits/<sha>/check-runs?per_page=100`, any workflow, any event); success only when there is at
    least one and every one completed with `success`; one queued or in progress waits, up to 20 minutes; any other
    conclusion (failure, cancelled, skipped, neutral) or a count that doesn't match refuses. The changes below keep
    `ci.yml`'s five jobs, on both pushes, so OPS-2's run sees 10 check runs as before, and the new workflow has no
    trigger but `workflow_dispatch`, so no push gives it a check run. Nothing here makes OPS-2's run wait or refuse,
    unless the new actions themselves fail CI, which the gate then reports as it should.
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: every job `runs-on: ubuntu-24.04` (what `ubuntu-latest` is until 2026-10-19);
    `actions/checkout@v7` (7.0.1, 2026-07-20), `actions/setup-java@v6` (6.0.1, 2026-09-09) and
    `actions/setup-node@v7` (7.0.0, 2026-07-14), each the latest release on GitHub, each `action.yml` at that tag
    saying `using: node24`; their release notes list no change that applies here (setup-node's v5 and v6 changed
    automatic caching, and the job names `cache: npm`). "Deploy scripts" checks out the whole history
    (`fetch-depth: 0`), for the cross-version case of OPS-2's last commit.
  - `.github/workflows/ci-ubuntu-26.yml` (new): "CI on Ubuntu 26.04", `workflow_dispatch` only, the jobs Backend,
    Frontend, Web image and Deploy scripts as `ci.yml` has them, on `ubuntu-26.04`. GitHub's runner reference lists
    `ubuntu-26.04` for public and private repositories, and runner-images issue 14748 says 26.04 is generally
    available and that `ubuntu-latest` moves to it from 2026-10-19, completing by 2026-11-19. Production's server
    runs Ubuntu 26.04.1 LTS (kernel 7.0.0-34).
  - D-44 in `requirements.md`; CLAUDE.md's CI line.

## 2026-10-03 · Family budget: no early-closed pipes in the checks; switch on checks the pages (F7b)

Commit "fix: no early-closed pipes in the checks; switch on checks the pages", F7b's fourth, whose hash belongs here
with the next change log commit. F7b's first three are `7046892`, `a01d9e4` and `3a6da31` ("ops: page checks, git
under umask 022, switch's text", the hash the entry below left open). Work on `feature/family-budget` at `3a6da31`
(equal to `main` and `origin/main`); not pushed, not merged, not deployed.

**What happened**
- The owner's `deploy/deploy.sh run 3a6da31… F7b` was REFUSED twice at "1.5 Preflight: CI" (run folders
  `/var/lib/finance-deploy/runs/2026-10-03T053414Z-3a6da31` and `2026-10-03T053626Z-3a6da31`): "Nothing changed: no
  merge, no image tagged, built or started". Production still runs `f0425c0` on V10, the switch off, `/privacy`
  answering 403.
- The cause: CI ran `3a6da31` twice. "Web image" passed in CI #45 (run 37100231413, the push to
  `feature/family-budget`) and failed in CI #46 (run 37100231426, the push to `main`; job 111138156378, step 3, the
  first `check-web-image.sh`) with "Process completed with exit code 141", 128 + SIGPIPE. The log wasn't read (no
  `gh` on the laptop, and GitHub's API wants a token for logs). The cause was found in the code instead:
  `asset=$(docker exec … ls /usr/share/nginx/html/assets | head -n 1)` under `set -euo pipefail`. `head` exits after
  the first name, and `docker exec`, still relaying, dies of SIGPIPE whenever it writes after that, which depends on
  timing. It was the one such pipe whose failure ended the script (an assignment under `set -e`). Run against stubs
  whose outputs exceed a pipe's buffer, `3a6da31`'s script exits 141 every time (below).

**Changes**
- Pipes into a reader that stops early, replaced by a capture first and a test after (or by a reader that reads to
  the end):
  - `.github/scripts/check-web-image.sh`: the check inside the image (was `printf "$inside" | grep -q` for the user,
    line 57, and `| grep -qv` for the problems, line 58), the assets' `ls | head -n 1` (line 97, CI #46), the headers'
    `curl -sI | tr | grep -qi` (line 101), and `docker logs | grep -F` and `| tail` (lines 76 and 109). Each output now
    goes to a file in `$work` and is read from there. The two on lines 57 and 58 couldn't end the script, but could
    give a wrong verdict: a false "user isn't nginx", or a false pass when a problem line came early.
  - `deploy/common.sh` `site_host` (line 262): `sed … | head -n 1` became `sed -nE '/…/{s//\1/p;q}'` on the file.
  - `deploy/deploy.sh` `compose_part` (line 116): `git show | awk '… { exit }'` became an awk that sets a flag and
    reads to the end.
  - The test harness: `in_order` and `in_order_file` in `deploy/tests/run.sh` (`grep -n | awk '… exit'`, lines 273
    and 383), and `stubs/pg-restore-test` (`sort | head -n 1 | cut`).
  - `page_checks` never piped: since `3a6da31` curl writes the body to a file with `-o`, and grep reads that file. Its
    comment now says why it must stay so.
  - Audited and left as they are, every reader reading to the end: `common.sh` lines 176, 233 and 295; `deploy.sh`'s
    CI check (`curl | python3`, which `json.load`s all of stdin), lines 95, 98, 209, 241, 243, 362 to 366, 461, 485,
    563, 695, 770 and 883; `rollback.sh` line 135 and every `} | tr '|' '/'`; `check-web-image.sh`'s `tar | tar`
    for `--server-modes`. The CI workflow's shell steps set no `shell:`, so they run as `bash -e` without pipefail, and
    their pipes (`mvnw | tee`, `sed | sed`) read to the end.
- Deterministic proofs:
  - `deploy/tests/stubs/curl`: a page answering 200 is about 1.4 MB, with its text near the start, made once per case.
    Without `-o` it writes the body to stdout, and it prints the status only with `-w`, as curl does. A new case,
    `large_pages_pass`, checks the size and `run`'s and `verify`'s pages. A new mutation, "read the page through
    `curl | grep -q`", puts that form into `page_checks`.
  - `.github/scripts/tests/check-web-image-test.sh` (new) with `stubs/docker` and `stubs/curl`: the script against
    stubs whose outputs (the check inside, the assets' listing, the headers, the pages, nginx's log) are each about
    1.4 MB with what the checks look for first. Two cases: every check passes, and one unreadable file reported as

## 2026-10-02 · Family budget: F7's deploy record, D-41, the web image's file modes, page checks in the deploy scripts (F7b)

Commits `7046892` (documents), `a01d9e4` (the web image), then "ops: page checks, git under umask 022, switch's text",
whose hash belongs here with the next change log commit. Work on `feature/family-budget`; not pushed, not merged, not
deployed.

**Changes**
- F7's deploy record and F7b's scope (commit "docs: F7 deploy, the file-mode cause, F7b scope"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": three rows for 2026-10-02. F7's deploy of `f0425c0` with every step of
    `deploy.sh run` as it passed, and the truth about the browser checks: failed (`/privacy` and `/privacy.html` 403),
    recorded as passed by mistake; `history` stays as written. `switch on` (run folder
    `2026-10-02T212036Z-switch-on`). `switch off` (D-41), whose summary wasn't at hand: the row names its run folder's
    `summary.txt` to copy in.
  - The cause, confirmed on the server: `deploy.sh` and `rollback.sh` set `umask 077`, so step 3.1's
    `git merge --ff-only` wrote the changed `frontend/public/privacy.html` with mode 600; the build carried the mode
    into the image (`-rw------- root root`), and nginx, running as uid 101, answered 403 for `/privacy` and
    `/privacy.html`. CI's and the laptop's checkouts give 644 or 664, so neither could see it. F6c's records (the
    runbook's row, the F6c change log entry, ADR 0003 topic J) said "unreproduced": they now give this cause, and why
    the second run passed (a manual `git checkout main` under umask 0022 rewrote the file as 644).
  - The runbook: the lesson (a 403, a 404 or wrong text on any page of a checklist means `no` in `finish`;
    `switch on` only after a clean `finish`); D-41 in "Switch the family budget on or off"; a troubleshooting row for
    a page answering 403 (the web container's root is read-only: only a deploy fixes it); a switch's run folder is
    named `…-switch-on` or `…-switch-off`, without the commit, as `start_log` names it.
  - D-41 (decided by the PM) in `requirements.md` and ADR 0003 topic J ("After the F7 deploy"), with F7b's scope and
    the client address corrected: F7's report claimed the `edge` network's subnet check proves D-29's per-address
    limit counts real clients; with Docker's IPv6 off, IPv6 clients arrive as the bridge's gateway (D-38), so the
    check proves nothing beyond the trusted range and is dropped. ADR 0003's F6a paragraph that planned it says so.
  - CLAUDE.md: production runs `f0425c0` on V10, with the switch off "until F7b's deploy".
  - The F7 change log entry below: `f0425c0`, its deploy record, its "Open" items.
- The web image readable whatever the checkout's modes (commit "fix: the web image readable whatever the checkout's
  modes"):
  - `frontend/Dockerfile`: the build stage sets every folder of `dist` to 0755 and every file to 0644 after
    `npm run build` (Vite copies `public/` with each file's mode); the `web` stage copies `web.conf` with
    `--chmod=0644`. `COPY` makes both root's. A single `--chmod` would give folders 0644 too, so `dist` gets its modes
    in the build stage. BuildKit, which `--chmod` needs, is what CI and the server (`docker compose build`, through
    bake) use; the api's Dockerfile needs it already, for `RUN --mount`.
  - `.github/scripts/check-web-image.sh` (new): builds the `web` target from a folder as it is, or with
    `--server-modes` from a copy whose files are 600 and folders 700, then checks the image inside as its own user
    (everything under `/usr/share/nginx/html` and `/etc/nginx/conf.d` readable by others, every file read through, the
    files root's) and through its own nginx: F7's checks of `/privacy`, `/privacy.html`, `/`, an SPA path, `/assets/`
    and the favicons, and no `Permission denied` in its log. Every failure is printed, not only the first.
  - CI's job "Web image" runs it twice, from the checkout and with `--server-modes`; ShellCheck covers it.
  - The audit of every file from the repository that a non-root process reads in production, built from a tree of
    600 files and 700 folders:
    - web (uid 101): before the fix, `/etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf` (`web.conf`) `-rw------- root`, so nginx
      stops at start (`[emerg] open() "/etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf" failed (13: Permission denied)`) and web would
      never be healthy; `privacy.html`, `privacy.css`, `favicon.ico` and `favicon.svg` `-rw-------`; `index.html` and
      `assets/` (written inside the build container) 644 and 755. After it, all 644, folders 755, root's.
    - api (`app`, uid 999): it reads `/app/app.jar` only, written by Maven inside the build container: `-rw-r--r--
      root` either way; the sources' modes never reach the image.
    - Bind mounts of `deploy/app/docker-compose.yml`: only `postgres-init.sh`, which the postgres image reads as
      uid 999 when it initializes a new volume. At 600: `docker-entrypoint.sh: line 188:
      /docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/10-finance.sh: Permission denied`, and the container exits 1. It is read only for a
      new volume, `deploy.sh` refuses a change to it, and it has been unchanged since the clone of 2026-09-28; from
      F7b's scripts on, no merge or checkout of theirs writes a file as 600.
- The deploy scripts (commit "ops: page checks, git under umask 022, switch's text"):
  - `deploy/common.sh`: `PAGES`, the one list of pages (`/` with `<div id="root">`, `/privacy` and `/privacy.html`
    with `<h1>Privacy policy</h1>`, both favicons, each 200); `site_host`, the host of `deploy/finance.caddy`'s site
    block, checked against a host name's shape and only ever an argument of `curl`; `page_checks`, a GET of each page
    through `https://` and that host, as users reach it, Caddy included; `git_tree`, a git command that writes the
    working tree under `umask 022` in a subshell, while everything else stays under 077.
  - `deploy.sh run`: step 3.1's merge through `git_tree`; a new step 3.6, the pages after health, whose failure is
    FAILED with the rollback command; every failure after the merge now says the site may still work but the deploy
    isn't good, not to run `finish`, and to send the output. The summary gains `Pages:`.
  - `deploy.sh finish`: the pages before the questions; if one fails, the browser checks are recorded as not passed,
    "the page checks failed: …", without asking, and only the smoke test is asked. The summary gains
    `Pages at finish:`, replaced like the answers when `finish` runs again.
  - `deploy.sh verify`: the pages; a failure is a problem.
  - `rollback.sh`: its checkout through `git_tree`; the pages after its restart, reported with a warning, never a
    reason to stop (the commit gone back to may hold the fault: `f0425c0`'s web image answers `/privacy` with 403);
    the summary gains `Pages:`.
  - `deploy.sh switch`: the confirmation says the family endpoints answer 200 to their members (F7's said "202"), and
    step 1.6 prints the numbers before, checked as `run`'s 1.7 checks them.
  - Which script runs F7b's own deploy: `f0425c0`'s `deploy.sh`, which bash reads before the merge. Its merge still
    runs under `umask 077`, so F7b's changed files land 600 (folders 700) in the server's clone, and it has no page
    step; F7b's `finish`, `verify` and `rollback.sh` are F7b's own. The `umask 022` rule shows from the next deploy
    after F7b, or in a rollback from F7b by its own `rollback.sh`. The image no longer cares (the fix above), and root
    reads the rest.
  - Tests (`deploy/tests/run.sh`, 63 cases): `run_fails_on_a_page`, `finish_records_failed_pages_without_asking`,
    `merged_files_land_644`, `rollback_reports_a_failing_page`, `switch_says_200_and_prints_the_numbers`; the stub of
    `curl` answers the site's pages (`fixtures/pages` gives a path another status); `verify_read_only` now allows
    the pages' GETs and counts them (5), and two CI cases count GitHub's calls only; `rollback_rolls_back` checks the
    pages after the restart. `deploy/tests/mutate.sh` (26): the page check dropped from `run`, `finish` asking
    despite a failed page check, `/privacy` dropped from the pages, git's `umask 022` dropped, "202" back in
    `switch`'s confirmation.
  - `deploy/checks/F7b.sql` and `F7b.expected`: F7's checks (Flyway's latest row, V10's column and functions), no
    family count pinned. `numbers.sql` unchanged.
  - The runbook ("Deploying with deploy.sh": step 3.6, the merge under `umask 022`, `finish`'s pages, `verify`, what
    the server needs, "File modes"; "Roll back with rollback.sh"; "Switch the family budget on or off") and
    CLAUDE.md describe them.

browser checks as passed by mistake while `/privacy` answered 403; `switch on` followed, and by D-41 the switch went
off again. The runbook's "Deployed revisions" has the record, and F7b's entry above the cause and the fix.

    `delete_family_ledger` function (as `F6c.sql` checks them), without a backslash and pinning no family count,
    since F7 turns the switch on. `numbers.sql` unchanged.
- The switch command (commit "ops: deploy.sh switch"):
  - `deploy/deploy.sh switch on|off`: preflight as `verify`'s (the read-only role, health) plus HEAD must be the last
    good deploy and the latest run must be finished, rolled back, adopted or an earlier switch, never mid-flight;
    shows the current `FAMILY_LEDGERS_ENABLED` line (never the rest of `.env`) and what it will become; asks for
    `SWITCH ON` or `SWITCH OFF` at the terminal (lines waiting there discarded first, as every question since F6c);
    copies `.env` beside itself, timestamped, mode 600; changes or adds only that one line, with its own safety check
    that nothing else in `.env` would change (refusing first, so a bug there can't touch a secret); restarts `api`
    alone (`docker compose up -d --no-deps api`, `web` untouched); checks health, the D-25 line against the value just
    written, and the numbers before and after (unchanged, since nothing in the database does); writes a `switch-on`
    or `switch-off` line in `history` and a summary for "Deployed revisions". No backup, no restore test: nothing in
    the database changes. On a failure after the change it prints the exact way back (the opposite direction) and
    stops; it never runs that command itself. `cmd_adopt`'s "nothing to adopt" case also recognizes `switch-on` and
    `switch-off` as HEAD's status.
  - `deploy/app/docker-compose.yml`'s `api` service now passes `FAMILY_LEDGERS_ENABLED` from `.env` through to the
    container (`${FAMILY_LEDGERS_ENABLED:-false}`, absent still meaning off, as today): it didn't before, so `switch`
    would have changed `.env` without the api ever seeing it. `deploy/app/.env.example` documents the line, commented
    out, as `switch` manages it. `run`, `verify` and `rollback.sh` needed no change: `switch_expected` already reads
    the api container's actual environment, not a cached value.
  - `run`'s confirmation now names the commit that is actually running (`BASE`, the last good deploy when it isn't
    HEAD) instead of HEAD: F6c's second run said "over `8ede02e`" while `7a60020` was running, after a rollback and a
    plain `git checkout main` left HEAD on `main`'s tip without updating the clone from origin.
  - Tests: `deploy/tests/run.sh` gains `switch_on_and_off`, `switch_wrong_confirmation`,
    `switch_secret_never_printed`, `switch_failure_prints_the_way_back` and `run_names_the_running_revision` (58
    cases); the stub `docker`'s `up` now recreates the `api` container, with its recorded environment and its log's
    D-25 line, when `FAMILY_LEDGERS_ENABLED` in the compose project's `.env` changes even though the image doesn't,
    while a plain deploy (the image changes) still uses the case's `start-log` and `env` fixtures exactly as before,
    D-25 line included, so every existing case is unaffected. `deploy/tests/mutate.sh` gains five mutations (21):
    `run`'s confirmation naming HEAD again, `switch`'s confirmation accepting anything, its `awk` matching by line
    position instead of by key (so it can rewrite another line, such as a secret), the environment printed while
    switching, and the way back not printed after a failure; one existing mutation ("let adopt refuse a finish-failed
    HEAD") updated for the `switch-on`/`switch-off` statuses `cmd_adopt`'s case now lists.

    discards the lines already waiting there and says how many; `finish`'s yes/no questions take only `yes` or `no`
    and ask again, and stop if the terminal closes without one.
  - `finish` works on the latest run that isn't refused (refused adopts included), which must be a deployed,
    finished or finish-failed deploy; it may run again, and the latest answers count in the summary (one line each),
    `last-good` (a `finish` line) and `history` (`good` or `finish-failed` as the newest line); not passed is
    `NOT PASSED: …` with exit status 3, not "an unexpected error".
  - A commit's status is its newest line in `history` (`commit_status`), shown in step 1.2 and by `adopt`, which now
    takes HEAD after `finish-failed` and has nothing to adopt only after `good`, `adopted` or `baseline`.
  - The rollback target, decided by the PM, one step back: the commit that HEAD's deploy replaced, as that run's
    `meta` recorded it (`base`), with its images from its newest line in `history` (not a `rolled-back-from` line), and
    under its tag; `deploy.sh`'s rollback command after a failure and `rollback.sh` use it. OPS-1's rule, which
    skipped a commit whose `finish` failed, is gone.
  - Step 1.3: a commit missing after `git fetch` says to push it from the laptop first; `rollback.sh` refused for
    the role names the runbook's "Roll an update back".
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`: the deploy block pasted alone with its answers typed, `finish` only after the browser checks
    and the smoke test and again if needed, the status by the newest line, `adopt`, and the rollback target. CLAUDE.md:
    the scripts and their tests in the project map.
  - `deploy/tests/`: F6b's scripts as committed in `7a60020` (`fixtures/f6b/`); production's state after F6b's deploy

  with its images, the database and `finance.conf` untouched. The cause, found after F7's deploy failed the same way
  (F7b's entry): F6b's script set `umask 077`, so its merge wrote the changed `frontend/public/privacy.html` with mode
  600, the web image kept it, and nginx (uid 101) couldn't read it. It looked unreproducible because every check used
  another image: production on `7a60020` (built from a 644 file) and `8ede02e`'s web image built on the laptop (a 664
  checkout) answered `/privacy` and `/privacy.html` with 200. After the rollback, `git checkout main` in an interactive
  shell rewrote the file as 644, and the second run's merge left it alone. The second
  run, by F6c's own script at 16:04:37Z after `git checkout main`, passed every check again, and this time the browser
  checks (the privacy page with the new section) and the smoke test passed; `finish` at 16:06:50Z, `verify F6c` at
  16:06:59Z: OK.

## 2026-10-02 · Family budget: OPS-1's deploy, D-35 to D-39, a read-only role for the deploy checks (F6b)

Commits `9cefaf4` (documents), `58779f5` (the backend), `5f00946` (the interface) and `7a60020` (the deploy scripts), on
`feature/family-budget`, merged into `main` and deployed on 2026-10-02 with `deploy/deploy.sh`, its first run (OPS-1's
script); the deploy's record: "docs: F6b deploy, F6c scope". The run deployed and passed its checks; `finish` ran before
the browser checks and the smoke test and took the next pasted block as its answers, so it recorded `finish-failed`; the
browser checks and the smoke test then passed, and `verify F6b` said OK (`deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions").

**Changes**
- OPS-1's deploy and the decisions after it (commit "docs: OPS-1 deploy, F6b scope, D-38 and D-39"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-10-02, `46dedcd` (OPS-1, no application change, the
    images still `c26cff6`'s, Flyway V9), with the owner's checks. The OPS-1 entry below got `46dedcd` and the deploy's
    record. CLAUDE.md: production runs `46dedcd` with the images of `c26cff6`.
  - The decisions after OPS-1's review, in the runbook and CLAUDE.md: the scripts' exception stays, with read-only a
    property of a database role; a cancelled CI run refuses the deploy; `deploy.sh adopt` closes a harmless stop and a
    manual deploy.
  - `requirements.md` and ADR 0003, each decided by the PM: D-35 clarified (a claim changes nothing about who takes
    part; a claimed seat takes part from the budget's start date), D-38 (the per-address limit only for public
    addresses, which settles F7's prerequisite about IPv6 clients; IPv6 in Docker in "Later"), D-39 (a returning member
    takes part from their return date only); F6 split into F6b and F6c.
- D-35 to D-39 in the backend (commit "feat(family): D-35 to D-39"), ADR 0003 topic J's "F6b as built":
  - V10 (`V10__claimed_seats_and_family_deletion.sql`, additive): `ledger_member.claimed_seat`, kept by a trigger (set
    when a seat gets its sub, cleared when a member who left returns), filled from the used claims (production has no
    family row); `delete_family_ledger(ledger)`; `release_family_memberships` calls it, otherwise as V9's.
  - D-35: a claimed seat takes part in records from the start date (`FamilyRecordService.joinedBy`): every split, the
    payer, the receiver and settlement sides may name them before the claim's date, and a record moved across it keeps
    them; only posting reads the claim's date, so the effect moves between the opening balance and the entries. Their own
    part before the claim's date has no account and no "Specify later" (422 `PAYMENT`, D-32).
    `FamilyMemberView.claimedSeat` (additive).
  - D-36: the last member with an account who leaves deletes the family budget through `delete_family_ledger`, after
    their detach; the archived state is gone (`ledger.archived_at`, from V5, stays unused).
  - D-37: the lookup's `entriesAfterReturn` (additive) and the 409 `ENTRIES_AFTER_RETURN` of accepting a return.
  - D-38: `InviteRateLimit` counts per address only a public one.
  - D-39: as built in F6a; tests.
  - `deploy/checks/F6b.sql` and `F6b.expected`: Flyway's latest row (V10), the family counts and claimed seats (0),
    V10's column, trigger and function, the release calling it, and F5's trigger and release as `OPS-1.sql` checks them.
    `numbers.sql` unchanged (no table added).
  - Tests: new `FamilyClaimedSeatApiTests` (2) and `FamilyReturnApiTests` (3); `FamilyClaimApiTests`: the D-31 test's
    last steps and the rule's test rewritten for D-35, as they pinned F6a's drop of the claimed seat;
    `FamilyMembershipApiTests`: the last member's leaving deletes the budget; `FamilyLifecycleRandomTests`: two rounds
    end with the budget gone, never archived; `FamilyClaimRandomTests`: half the new records name the seat, three date
    changes in four cross the claim's date, 35 operations a round; `FamilyInvariants`: no family ledger without an ACTIVE
    member with an account; `LedgerSchemaTests` and `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests` with V10; `ClientAddressTests` (2
    new), `ClientAddressUntrustedPeerTests` (its loopback peer has no per-address limit now) and `InviteRateLimitTests`
    (1 new) for D-38; `DataIsolationApiTests.aReturnsOwnEntriesAndAClaimedSeatAreTheMembersOwn` (new; nothing else in
    the file changed).
- The interface (commit "feat(frontend): D-35 to D-39, two fixes"), ADR 0003 topic J's "F6b as built":
  - D-35: `expenseForm.takesPart` and `inOpeningBalance`; the new-record, settlement and record-page forms take a
    claimed seat in a split or as payer before the claim's date, and their own record or side there without an account,
    with a line why. `FamilyMember.claimedSeat` in `api.ts` (missing counts as false).
  - D-36: the leave confirmation of the last member with an account says the budget and its records will be deleted,
    and its button "Leave and delete the family budget" (`family.closesBudget`).
  - D-37: the invite page lists a return's own entries after it (date with a link to the entry, what it adds to the
    debt, memo), keeps Accept disabled, and looks the invite up again on "Check again" and on a 409
    `ENTRIES_AFTER_RETURN`.
  - The switcher right after accepting an invite or leaving: `useApi.update` puts the budget just joined into its list,
    or takes the one just left out, before the list has loaded again; leaving navigates first, and the update is a
    transition, so that it commits with the router's navigation.
  - The members table at 375 px: under 40rem each member is a block of labelled lines (`data-label`), with the actions
    wrapping below.
  - Tests: new `FamilyF6b.test.tsx` (7); `family.test.ts`: D-36's sentence and `closesBudget`.
- The deploy scripts (commit "ops: a read-only role for checks, deploy.sh adopt"):

    (`PGOPTIONS` still asks for read-only transactions too); `require_checks_role` reads the role's own description

    drop a wrong role), "Adopt the running revision", and the role and `adopt` where the deploy section names them.

## 2026-10-01 · Family budget: F5's deploy, D-29 to D-34, the membership's lifecycle (F6a)

Commits `12c0af3` (documents), `dc99b2b` (leaving, removal, the rule back to equal shares), `7dd43ea`
(return, owners, what "Delete all my data" touches) and `c26cff6` (leaving, removal, return, owners in
the interface), on `feature/family-budget`, merged into `main` and deployed on 2026-10-02; the deploy's
records: "docs: F6a deploy, OPS-1 scope, D-35 to D-37".

**Changes**
- F5's deploy and the decisions after it (commit "docs(family-budget): F5 deploy, F6a scope, D-29 to
  D-34"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-10-01, `e8f5ca0` (F5, Flyway V9, switch
    off), with the owner's checks. The F5 entry below got its commits and the deploy's record.
  - `docs/family-budget/requirements.md`: new D-29 to D-34, each decided by the PM: the 409s for a valid
    invite the user can't use and the rate limits, once the client address is shown to be real (D-29); a
    joining member's category of the other type stays private (D-30); records before a claimed member's
    join date stay editable, with the opening balances in step (D-31); a member who took a seat stays in
    its earlier records (D-32); merged categories are deleted, so the detach creates personal copies
    (D-33); a claim's opening balance on the invite (D-34). The planned stages: F6 split into F6a and
    F6b. "Later": pending invites for a taken seat, folding a long invite history, one address for a
    household.
  - ADR 0003: the status line (F5 deployed from `e8f5ca0`); topic E's detach as D-33 changes it; topic
    G, the decisions D-29 to D-34; topic J, F6 split and the "F6a plan": who may leave, remove, return
    and make owners, which rows change in which ledgers, what posts, and how D-10 holds afterwards.
  - CLAUDE.md: production runs `e8f5ca0` on Flyway V9; decisions up to D-34; F6a and F6b.
- Leaving, removal and the rule back to equal shares, with the F5 follow-ups (commit "feat(family):
  leaving, removal, the rule back to EQUAL"), ADR 0003 topic J's "F6a as built". No migration.
  - `DELETE /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/members/me` (new): any member with an account leaves. `DELETE
    /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/members/{memberId}` now removes any member, with or without an
    account (owners only). The member becomes LEFT on today's date, a MEMBER, without a custom share;
    one without an account whom no record names, and without a custom share, is deleted as before. The
    last owner while another member with an account remains: 409 with `code` `LAST_OWNER` (new additive
    property of a 409). The last member with an account leaving archives the family budget.
  - New `FamilyMembershipService`; the detach through `CrossLedgerWriter.detach` (links detached, a
    personal category for each family category the member's personal ledger refers to, D-33, with
    `_2` where D-30's private category has the code; `Debt(L)` an ordinary liability that keeps its
    balance). Nothing is posted. Pending invites of the member and for a removed seat's place are
    revoked. A custom share above 0 puts the rule back to EQUAL, journaled as `SPLIT_RULE_RESET`.
    `FamilyMemberView.leftDate` (additive).
  - "Today": new `ledger.Today` for the start date, the join dates and the leave date (the api's zone,
    UTC in production, which is also the database session's); a claim's join date may be left out for
    the server's today, as a start date may.
  - D-34: the lookup's `openingBalance` for a claim. The privacy draft lists what the lookup shows, and
    says what leaving and removal do.
  - The client address: analysed (in the report and the ADR), no change needed; `InviteRateLimit`'s
    Javadoc names the real path.
  - Tests: new `FamilyMembershipApiTests` (7), `ClientAddressTests` (2) and
    `ClientAddressUntrustedPeerTests` (1); `FamilyClaimApiTests.theOpeningBalanceFollowsRecordsAcrossTheJoinDate`
    (new) and the lookup's opening balance; `DataIsolationApiTests.aMemberWhoLeftOrWasRemovedReadsNothingOfTheBudget`
    (new; nothing else in the file changed); `FamilyInvariants` checks that a member who left is
    detached; `IntegrationTest` has a `TestClock`; `FamilyLedgerApiTests`, `FamilyRecordApiTests` and
    `FamilyInviteApiTests` expect the new removal and the optional join date; `ApiTests` and
    `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` (31 endpoints) with the new endpoint.
- Return, owners and what "Delete all my data" touches (commit "feat(family): return, owners, what
  Delete all my data touches"), ADR 0003 topic J's second "F6a as built". No migration.
  - Return (D-26): an invite for a new member brings a member who left back, ACTIVE from today, with
    the same debt account linked again (`CrossLedgerWriter.relinkDebt`), their categories merged as at
    the first join, every record from today, and one `FAMILY_CORRECTION` on the join date: their family
    balance before it less what their debt account shows from entries not posted now. Every re-post
    keeps it in step, as it keeps a claimed member's opening balance. Their entries of before they left
    for records dated from the join date (the same day, or later) are attached again
    (`CrossLedgerWriter.reattach`) rather than posted anew, so nothing counts twice. A claim held by a
    member who left stays 409. The lookup gains `returning` and `correction` (additive).
  - `EntryService`: `PUT` and `DELETE` of an entry that posts to a debt account naming a family budget
    (a returned member's entries of before they left) answer 409.
  - `POST /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/members/{memberId}/owner` (new, owners only).
  - `GET /api/me/family-memberships` (new, behind the switch, `FamilyMembershipsController`): each family
    budget the caller is an ACTIVE member of, with role, balance, outcome (`STAYS`, `OWNERSHIP_PASSES`
    with the new owner's name, `DELETED`), pending invites and the split rule's reset; how many they
    left. The deletion itself is unchanged.
  - The privacy draft: coming back, and what the confirmation screen lists.
  - Tests: `FamilyMembershipApiTests` (4 new: the return's worked example, a return on the same day,
    owners, the preview); new
    `FamilyLifecycleRandomTests`; `UserDataApiTests.deletingAllMyDataDoesWhatItsPreviewSaid` (new);
    `DataIsolationApiTests.theDeletionPreviewAndNewOwnersAreTheMembersOwn` (new; nothing else in the
    file changed); `FamilyInviteApiTests` expects a member who left to be returning, and a claim of
    theirs refused; `ApiTests` and `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` (33 endpoints) with the two new ones.
- Leaving, removal, return and owners in the interface (commit "feat(frontend): leaving, removal,
  return, owners"), ADR 0003 topic I ("F6a as built: the interface"):
  - The members page: Leave on the reader's own row, Remove on other members' rows and Make owner for
    owners, "Left on" for members who left; Leave and Remove open a confirmation with the member's
    balance, what stays in their personal budget, the records that freeze, the split rule back to equal
    shares and a budget that closes; the last owner is told to make another owner first, and the
    server's 409 `LAST_OWNER` shows too. After leaving, the switcher reloads and the dashboard opens.
  - The invite page: a claim's opening balance (D-34) and a returning member's correction (D-26).
  - "Invite to take this place" leaves the join date out when it is the browser's today.
  - "Delete all my data", with the switch on, lists each family budget the user is in, with role,
    balance and what happens to it, and how many they left.
  - Tests: new `FamilyLifecycle.test.tsx` (10); `family.test.ts` (5 new); `Family.test.tsx` and
    `Invite.test.tsx` updated for the confirmation and the page's request for the balances.

## 2026-10-01 · Family budget: F4e's deploy and review, invites and taking a seat (F5)

Commits `29af12b` (documents), `0a1f990` (invites and taking a seat) and `e8f5ca0` (invites in the
interface), on `feature/family-budget`, merged into `main` and deployed on 2026-10-01; the deploy's
records: "docs(family-budget): F5 deploy, F6a scope, D-29 to D-34".

**Changes**
- F4e's deploy and the decisions after its review (commit "docs(family-budget): F4e deploy, F5
  scope, privacy draft"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-10-01, `31419bd`, with the owner's
    checks. The F4e entry below got its commit `31419bd` and the deploy's record.
  - ADR 0003: the status line (F4e deployed from `31419bd`); topic E, kept after the F4e review: the
    acting member's manual rate shown as "manual rate", the original currency from the request (the
    forms send the account's), no conversion for an account change in the same currency, the lock
    over the currency and the base amount, and `accountAmount` only for an account in another
    currency than the base; topic F: D-11 amended for joining members; topic J: F5 is invites and
    taking a seat, and returning members (D-26) and making another member an owner move to F6.
  - `docs/family-budget/requirements.md`: D-11 amended (decided by the owner): a joining member's
    personal category with a family category's code merges into it and the family name stays;
    renaming stays an owner's right (D-15); the acceptance screen shows each merge in advance; other
    personal categories may be brought, the rest stay private. The planned stages: F5 and F6 as
    above.
  - New `docs/family-budget/privacy-draft.md`, the privacy policy's draft for family budgets, to be
    published in F6, with a visible TODO for a legal review.
  - CLAUDE.md: production runs `31419bd`.
- Invites and taking a seat in the backend (commit "feat(family): invites and taking a seat"), D-17
  and D-18 as the task decided them, ADR 0003 topic G ("F5 as built"), with what differs from that
  topic's sketch. Part 3 of the task, what a claim does to existing records, is in the same commit:
  the claim's posting can't be split from the acceptance. Migration V9, additive.
  - V9: `ledger_invite` (the token's SHA-256 only, a claim's seat and join date, the creator, the
    expiry at most 7 days on, and one final end: revoked, used by a membership, or declined), its
    trigger, and `release_family_memberships` again, which revokes the member's pending invites and
    deletes a family ledger's invites with it (D-20).
  - Owners (`FamilyInviteController`, behind the switch): POST, GET and DELETE
    `/api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/invites`. The creation's answer alone holds the link,
    `<app.public-url>/invite#<token>` (new property `app.public-url`, env `APP_PUBLIC_URL`, production's
    address by default; the local compose files set theirs). At most 20 pending; 409 for a seat with an
    account; 422 `JOIN_DATE`.
  - The holder: POST `/api/invites/lookup`, `/accept` and `/decline`, the token in the body only. One
    404 for a token that lets nobody in, whatever the reason; 409 for a valid one the user can't use
    (a member already, a member who left, the seat taken). Rate limited per user and per client
    address, 10 a minute and 50 an hour each (`InviteRateLimit`, 429 with `Retry-After`, new
    `api.TooManyRequestsException`).
  - `ledger.access.LedgerInvites`: the way in by invite, before there is a membership, and the only read
    of a family ledger for a non-member (what the lookup shows); a new `ArchitectureTests` exception
    with its reason. `CurrentUser` gains the account's `name`, which prefills the display name.
  - Accepting (`FamilyInviteService`): the membership (a claim's seat with its history, or a new
    member today), the categories as D-11 now says (same code and type merged as at creation, the
    family name kept; the other type kept private; the brought ones made family categories), then
    `FamilyPostingService.join`: the debt account, every record from the join date (shares; payments,
    receipts and settlement sides of the seat on the placeholder, in dollars through `FX_EXCHANGE`),
    and the balance before the join date as one `FAMILY_OPENING` entry.
  - Found while building it, decided and recorded in topic G: every re-post keeps the opening balances
    of members who joined after the start date in step, since a record before a member's join date can
    still change; and a member who took a seat stays in the records of their time without an account
    (their payment fields theirs, an account for them 422 `PAYMENT`), where `JOINED_AFTER` would have
    frozen those records for everyone.
  - Tests: new `FamilyInviteApiTests` (10), `FamilyClaimApiTests` (4), `FamilyClaimRandomTests` and
    `InviteRateLimitTests` (3); `LedgerSchemaTests.invitesFitTheirFamilyLedger` (new);
    `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests` pins V8 and adds a V9 step;
    `DataIsolationApiTests.invitesLetTheirHoldersIntoTheirOwnFamilyBudgetOnly` (new; nothing else in
    the file changed); `UserDataApiTests.deletingAllMyDataRevokesMyPendingInvites` (new); `ApiTests`
    and `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` (30 endpoints) with the six new endpoints; `FamilyInvariants`
    compares a debt account with the family balance from the member's join date on (D-10);
    `LedgerApiTest`'s owned rows include the invites.
  - `deploy/pg-backup/finance.conf`: `CHECK_FAMILY_INVITES`. The runbook: the restore test expects 19
    tables and migration 9; "Delete a user" says the release revokes pending invites.
- Invites in the interface (commit "feat(frontend): invites and taking a seat"), ADR 0003 topic I ("F5
  as built: invites in the interface"):
  - `invite.ts` (no React): the token from `/invite#<token>`, the fragment removed from the address bar
    before anything else, the token in `localStorage` for 7 days, `/invite` opened once the app knows
    the user while a token waits (also in the tab a sign-in or a registration's verification email ends
    in), erased with the switch off and at logout; a link opened in a tab on `/invite` already is taken
    on `hashchange`. `main.tsx` and `auth.ts` call it.
  - `Invite.tsx` (`/invite`, with the switch on): the lookup in words, what the others will and won't
    see, the categories that merge, stay private or may come along, the name prefilled from the
    account; Accept opens the budget and reloads the switcher; Decline; the one message for an invalid
    token with "Back to Personal"; a taken name at its field; "Try again later" for a 429, keeping the
    token. The token is erased after every other outcome.
  - `FamilyInvites.tsx` on the members page, for owners: "Invite someone new", "Invite to take this
    place" with a join date, the new link once with a copy button, the invites with their status and
    Revoke. A claimed seat's opening balance reads as one in the personal pages (`EntryEditor.tsx`).
  - Tests: new `invite.test.ts` (9) and `Invite.test.tsx` (12); `FamilyRows.test.tsx` (the opening
    balance's entry); `Family.test.tsx`'s members test compares the writes only, since an owner's
    members page also loads the invites.

## 2026-10-01 · Family budget: F4d's deploy and review, the settlement lock, other currencies (F4e)

Commits `7d5cef6` (documents), `915fe30` (the settlement lock), `97766c4` (other currencies) and
`31419bd` (other currencies in the family pages), on `feature/family-budget`, merged into `main` and
deployed on 2026-10-01; the deploy's records: "docs(family-budget): F4e deploy, F5 scope, privacy
draft".

**Changes**
- F4d's deploy and the decisions after its review (commit "docs(family-budget): F4d deploy,
  settlement lock, runbook"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-10-01, `144ff6b`, with the owner's
    checks. The F4d entry below got its commit `144ff6b` and the deploy's record.
  - `docs/family-budget/requirements.md`: new D-28, the settlement lock: once the other side of a
    settlement, a member with an account, has put its part on an account of theirs, the
    settlement's date and amount don't change and it isn't deleted (409, naming the member and
    saying they can move their part back to "Specify later"); it replaces F4d's move of their part
    back to the placeholder. "Later": the paths `/family/{id}/expenses…` become `activity…`, with
    redirects.
  - ADR 0003: the status line (F4d deployed from `144ff6b`); topic E, changed after the F4d review:
    the lock replaces "Only a member posts to their own account"; kept as built: `POST
    /settlements` with reading and changes through `/records`, and no private note on a settlement
    side; topic I, changed: the settlement page explains the lock.
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`: the blocks of "Update the app" (and of its copy in the migration-only
    release) print the commit they read from `/root/finance-tracker.previous` before they use it;
    "Roll an update back" first prints it in a read-only block, to be checked against "Deployed
    revisions", then uses it.
  - CLAUDE.md: production runs `144ff6b`; the rule against variables filled from files applies to
    the commands the agent runs and writes into deploy checklists, where commits are explicit,
    while the runbook's generic blocks for the human operator may read
    `/root/finance-tracker.previous`; decisions up to D-28.
- The settlement lock, D-28 (commit "feat(family): lock a settlement once the other side has placed
  its part"), ADR 0003 topic E ("F4e as built: the settlement lock"). No migration.
  - A settlement's new date or amount, and its deletion, answer 409 while its other side (the side
    that didn't record it, a member with an account) has its part on an account of theirs: "Dad has
    put their side of this settlement on an account of theirs, so its date and amount can't change;
    Dad can move it back to "Specify later" to allow it." (or "so it can't be deleted"), also through
    the recorder's own side's entry. Its comment still changes; the recorder's own account and a side
    without an account never lock it.
  - The record's answer gains `lockedBy` (the other side, additive), only for who would otherwise
    change it; `canEditPayment` and `canDelete` are false while it is set.
  - No path writes another member's own account any more: `FamilyPostingService.post` keeps a side
    on its member's own account as it is when anyone else acts and throws if a re-post would rewrite
    or delete it, and `CrossLedgerWriter.replace` and `delete` refuse an entry that isn't
    `system_owned` unless its member acts. F4d's move of the other side's part back to the
    placeholder is gone.
  - The interface: the recorder's settlement page says who locked it and why, without the date,
    amount and delete; the comment stays. The other side's page and their personal entry say that
    their account locks it and that "Specify later" unlocks it. The recorder's side entry shows the
    lock with the date and amount disabled and no delete. F4d's line about the other side's part
    moving back is gone.
  - Tests: `FamilySettlementApiTests` (the first test now expects the lock and its release, and the
    new `theLockFollowsTheOtherSidesPartOnly`); `CrossLedgerWriterTests.anotherMembersSideOnTheirOwnAccountIsBeyondReach`
    (new); `DataIsolationApiTests.aSettlementsLockNamesItsOtherSideToItsRecorderOnly` (new; nothing
    else in the file changed); `FamilyRecordRandomTests` tracks whether the other side placed its
    part and expects the 409s; `FamilyRecords.test.tsx` (4 new or rewritten).
- Other currencies in the backend (commit "feat(family): other currencies"), C7 and D-13, ADR 0003
  topic D ("Decided for F4e") and topic E ("F4e as built: other currencies"). Migration V8, additive.
  - Requests: `POST /records`, `POST /settlements` and `PATCH /records/{recordId}` take `currency`
    and `baseAmount` (additive); `amount` is the original amount, in the base currency when
    `currency` is left out, as before. The PATCH and `PATCH /api/entries/{id}/family-payment` also
    take `accountAmount`, for the other side of a settlement whose account is in another currency.
  - The base amount: the original amount in the base currency; else the request's `baseAmount`
    (`ENTERED`); else the ECB's rate on the date or the latest before it (`ECB`), or where the ECB has
    none, the acting member's own manual rate (`MANUAL`); else 422 `RATE_MISSING`. HALF_UP to the
    base currency's minor unit, from the two euro rates (`RateService.recordRate`, `RecordRate`).
    New codes: `RATE_MISSING`, `BASE_AMOUNT`, `ACCOUNT_AMOUNT`.
  - Answers: a record adds `originalAmount`, `originalCurrency`, and where converted `rate`,
    `rateSource` and `rateDate`; `yourPayment` adds the side's own `amount` and `currency`. New: `GET
    /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/conversion?amount=&currency=&date=`, the base amount a record of the
    caller would get, with their own rates only.
  - Posting: a payment, receipt or settlement side in another currency than the base goes through the
    member's `FX_EXCHANGE` (rule 9), the debt account always in the base currency. The other side's
    own amount on a foreign account lives only on its entry. V8's guard allows `FX_EXCHANGE` only for
    those kinds and only in the acting member's own ledger; `CrossLedgerWriter` checks the same.
  - Edits: a new original amount, currency or date converts the base amount again unless
    `baseAmount` is given; a new currency needs its amount; splits and postings follow; the journal
    adds `originalAmount` ("56.00 USD→67.20 USD"), never an account or a side's own amount.
  - V8: `family_record.base_rate`, `base_rate_source`, `base_rate_date`, a check and a trigger
    keeping them consistent with the currencies, and `posting_family_guard` with the `FX_EXCHANGE`
    branch (otherwise V7's word for word).
  - The integrity check's family row counts the debt account in the base currency only.
  - Tests: new `FamilyCurrencyApiTests` (6); `LedgerSchemaTests.recordsInOtherCurrenciesAndTheirExchange`
    (new); `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests` pins V7 and adds a V8 step;
    `DataIsolationApiTests.aConversionAndASidesOwnAmountAreTheMembersOwn` (new; nothing else in the
    file changed); `FamilyRecordRandomTests` with dollars in the mix; `FamilyInvariants` checks the
    debt accounts' currency; `ApiTests` and `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` (24 endpoints) with the new
    endpoint; `FamilyPaymentApiTests` and `FamilySettlementApiTests` expect `yourPayment`'s new
    fields.
- Other currencies in the interface (commit "feat(frontend): other currencies in the family pages"),
  ADR 0003 topic I ("F4e as built: other currencies in the interface"):
  - `currency.ts` (no React) and `FamilyCurrency.tsx`: the rate line ("1 USD = 0.851064 EUR, ECB rate
    of Sep 30, 2026", six significant digits with big.js), a record's two amounts ("$58.75 →
    €50.00"), the base amount of a form (the same currency, converted by `GET /conversion`, typed in,
    missing or pending), "Amount in EUR" with its rate and source and "Use the exchange rate", and a
    currency field.
  - The expense and income forms, the settlement form, the edit forms of a record's page and of a
    settlement, `PaymentEntry.tsx` and the personal editor's family option: the amount in the
    account's currency (an account with a currency decides it; otherwise a currency field), the
    split of the base amount, `currency` and `baseAmount` sent only when needed. Without a rate, the
    field says why and saving waits; a 422 `RATE_MISSING` lands at the field.
  - "Settle up" on an account in another currency keeps what settles as the base amount and asks
    for the amount paid. The other side of a settlement choosing an account in another currency is
    asked for its own amount (`accountAmount`), which its side then shows for its eyes only.
  - Lists, the overview and record pages show both amounts and the rate; the journal names "the
    amount paid" and "the amount in EUR". The personal editor's family option takes any currency
    (F4c's "other currencies come later" line is gone).
  - Tests: new `currency.test.ts` (8) and `FamilyCurrencies.test.tsx` (9); `FamilyPayer.test.tsx`
    replaces the other-currency refusal with two tests (an entry in dollars converted, one in
    roubles without a rate); fixtures gain the new fields.

**Verified**
- Baseline, on the laptop in `~/dev/finance-tracker` after `git fetch origin`:
  `feature/family-budget` checked out with a clean tree; it, `main` and `origin/main` all at
  `144ff6b`. `./mvnw -B clean verify`: 335 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped, BUILD SUCCESS.
  Frontend: `npm ci`, `npm test` 16 files and 220 tests passed, `npm run build` succeeded.
- After the documents' commit: documents only.
- After the lock's commit: `./mvnw -B clean verify` 338 tests (335 and 3), 0 failures, 0 errors, 0
  skipped, BUILD SUCCESS; the invariants after every family test and after each of the random
  test's 240 operations (from the seed: 73 expenses and 21 incomes created, 34 changes, 23 payment
  edits, 29 deletes, 12 split rules, 20 settlements, 16 settlement changes, 8 settlement deletes,
  and 4 refused by the lock). Frontend: `npm test` 16 files and 224 tests passed (220 before),
  `npm run build` succeeded.
- `aSettlementsLockNamesItsOtherSideToItsRecorderOnly` against a temporary mutation, from a file
  copy: `lockedBy` computed for every reader made it fail (Bob's answer held it). The file restored.
- After the currencies' commit: `./mvnw -B clean verify` 346 tests (338 and 8), 0 failures, 0
  errors, 0 skipped, BUILD SUCCESS; the invariants after every family test and after each of the
  random test's 240 operations (from the seed: 74 expenses and 29 incomes created, 26 changes, 25
  payment edits, 30 deletes, 10 split rules, 21 settlements, 17 settlement changes, 6 settlement

- `aConversionAndASidesOwnAmountAreTheMembersOwn` against a temporary mutation, from a file copy:
  `findLatestManual` reading any user's manual rate made it fail (Bob's conversion used Alice's
  rate). The file restored.
- Rollback evidence for V8: in a scratch worktree of `144ff6b` with only V8 added (the same file by
