  that pins the schema: `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests` expects version 7 as the latest. Flyway applied
  V8; every API, isolation, delete-all, runbook, importer, report and demo test passed. (A first run
  had found V8's table check named like the column check of `base_rate`; renamed, then run again.)
- After the interface's commit: frontend `npm test` 18 files and 241 tests passed (224 before),
  `npm run build` succeeded; the backend unchanged since the currencies' commit.
- The walk-through in headless Chrome (playwright-core), against the backend on the laptop with
  `FAMILY_LEDGERS_ENABLED=true` and `ECB_RATES_ENABLED=false` on a throwaway `postgres:17-alpine`
  (with three ECB dollar rates inserted for 2026-09-26 to 09-30), the Vite dev server and the dev
  Keycloak's testuser, after "Delete all my data" and the demo (its "US dollar account"), with
  "Home" in EUR from 2026-09-01 (Groceries and Salary brought in), Sam without an account, a rouble
  account and the user's own rate of 100 roubles to the euro:
  1. An expense of 58.75 USD from the dollar account: "Amount in EUR" 50.00, "1 USD = 0.851064
     EUR, ECB rate of Sep 30, 2026", no currency field; the page "$58.75 → €50.00"; the payment
     dollar account −58.75 USD, `FX_EXCHANGE` +58.75 USD and −50.00 EUR, debt +50.00 EUR.
  2. "Specify later" in USD, 23.50: €20.00, the placeholder through `FX_EXCHANGE` the same way.
  3. "Settle up" (Sam owed 35.00) received into the dollar account: the 35.00 moved to "Amount in
     EUR", the dollars asked; 41.13 USD recorded, "Sam paid you €35.00", both balances 0.00.
  4. From the personal editor, a family income of 9000 RUB into the rouble account: €90.00 at the
     user's own rate, shares 45.00 each, the receipt through `FX_EXCHANGE`.
  5. The lock: Pat, a member with an account, added with plain SQL as the tests do; the user paid
     Pat 10.00 from Cash; Pat's part moved from Pat's placeholder to a cash account of Pat's with SQL
     as the posting service's writer would. The settlement page: the note naming Pat, no date,
     amount or delete, the comment still changes; `PATCH` of the amount and `DELETE` 409 with D-28's
     message; the user's own side entry with the date and amount disabled and no delete.
  After each step `/api/reports/integrity` read `[]`. At 375 px the activity, the expense and income
  forms, the settlement form, four record pages, balances, journal, overview, the personal editor

  up" read its base-currency amount as dollars on a dollar account (now it moves to "Amount in EUR",
  with a test); the edit forms showed a currency field for a payment on an account with its own
  currency. Rechecked. The console's errors: `/api/me`'s 401 before the sign-in and the script's own
  two 409s. The instance, its database and the dev Keycloak were stopped; screenshots stay in the
  session's scratchpad.

**Open**
- The owner's deploy of F4e with the switch off; the report has the checklist. V8 runs on deploy; no
  `finance.conf` or `finance.caddy` change. Rollback: the `:previous` images, `144ff6b`, which the
  rollback evidence above shows running on V8.
- The original currency is the request's, by default the base currency; the server doesn't take it
  from the paying account, whose currency is only a default in this data model, and an unchanged
  isolation check pays a EUR income into a USD account. The forms send the account's currency.
- The other side of a settlement names an amount only for an account whose default currency isn't
  the base; on an account without a currency its side is in the base currency.
- An account change alone keeps a record's currency (it converts nothing) unless the form sends a
  new currency with a new amount, as it does for an account with another currency.
- A record's page says "manual rate" for a base amount converted at its author's own rate, to every
  member, the author included; a form's conversion says "your own rate", since it is the reader's.

## 2026-10-01 · Family budget: F4c's deploy and review, settlements and incomes (F4d)

Commits `82d5f7c` (documents), `469eba4` (settlements), `567bf8e` (family incomes) and `144ff6b`
(incomes, settlements and settling up in the interface), on `feature/family-budget`, merged into
`main` and deployed on 2026-10-01; the deploy's records: "docs(family-budget): F4d deploy,
settlement lock, runbook".

**Changes**
- F4c's deploy and the decisions after its review (commit "docs(family-budget): F4c deploy and
  review decisions"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-09-30, `e7cdeb1`, with the owner's
    checks. The F4c entry below got its commit `e7cdeb1` and the deploy's record, and lost a stray
    blank line in its "Verified".
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`: every restore test comes right after a fresh backup, in the same block
    (section 9's test now starts with `systemctl start pg-backup@finance.service`, and "Restore
    from a backup" dumps the current state before it tests the chosen dump), with a note that a
    restore test against an older dump fails after any change in production, as it did in F4c's
    deploy; the troubleshooting row for `MISMATCH` says to back up and test again in one block.
  - ADR 0003: the status line (F4c deployed from `e7cdeb1`); topic E, kept after the F4c review: the
    payer's private note on their own payment entry, and equal shares split again among the record's
    own members as of the new date; topic I, changed: the expense page's split preview follows the
    record's stored split, not the budget's current rule (fixed in F4d).
  - CLAUDE.md: production runs `e7cdeb1`.
- Settlements (commit "feat(family): settlements"), D2 and D-24, ADR 0003 topic E ("F4d as built:
  settlements"). No migration: V7 has the type, the payee, the link and the kind.
  - `POST /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/settlements` (new, only with the switch on; 201): `date`,
    `amount`, `payerMemberId`, `payeeMemberId`, `comment`, and for the recorder's own side
    `paymentAccountId` or `paymentLater`. The answer is the record (type `SETTLEMENT`, category,
    split method and shares empty) with the new `payee`; settlements are listed, read, changed and
    deleted through `/records` like expenses.
  - Who: a member with an account who pays or receives; an owner, between two members without an
    account ("Only an owner of the family budget records a settlement between two members without an
    account.", 409). A member with an account named by someone else: 422 `PAYER` or `PAYEE` (new
    code); the same member twice: `PAYEE`; a side with an account who joined after the date:
    `JOINED_AFTER`; before the start date: 409.
  - Posting (`FAMILY_SETTLEMENT`, link `SETTLEMENT`): the payer's account −x and debt +x, the
    receiver's account +x and debt −x. The recorder's side on the account they name or their
    placeholder; the other side, with an account, on their "Payments without a specified account",
    which they move to an account through `PATCH /records/{recordId}` (`paymentAccountId`,
    `paymentLater`) or `PATCH /api/entries/{id}/family-payment` (`accountId`, `later`), without a new
    version or a journal line; nothing for a side without an account.
  - Changes: the recorder (between members without an account, its author or an owner) changes the
    date, amount and comment and deletes it, also through their own side's entry; a category, split,
    other payer or note is 422; frozen and stale are 409 ("Settlement 2 has changed since version
    0. …", "The settlement is frozen: …", "Settlement 2 not found."). A change of the date or amount
    moves the other side's part back to their placeholder if they had put it on an account of
    theirs: only its member posts to their own account (D-8).
  - The journal: creation (date, amount, payer, `payee`, comment), changes, deletion; its record
    summary gains `type`. `yourPayment` is each side's own.
  - The posting service posts sides generally (the expense's payment is the payer's side); the
    personal endpoints: `DELETE /api/entries/{id}` of one's own side deletes the settlement if one
    recorded it (else 409 naming the recorder), `PUT` answers 409 ("Entry N is your side of a
    settlement in the family budget "Home"; change it there."). `ledger.FamilyPayments.deleteExpenseOf`
    is now `deleteRecordOf`. The expense messages are unchanged apart from the payment endpoint's
    "Entry N is not your payment for a family expense or your side of a settlement, …".
  - Tests: new `FamilySettlementApiTests` (3); `DataIsolationApiTests.aSettlementSideIsItsMembersOwn`
    (new; the file gained 106 lines and lost none); `FamilyRecordRandomTests` with settlements in the
    mix; `CrossLedgerWriterTests` refuses her cash and a note on her side of a settlement;
    `FamilyInvariants` computes balances with incomes and settlements (topic D's formula) and checks
    that no settlement has shares; `ApiTests` (401 and the OpenAPI paths) and
    `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` (23 endpoints) with the new endpoint.
- Family incomes (commit "feat(family): family incomes"), C5, ADR 0003 topic E ("F4d as built:
  incomes"). No migration.
  - `POST /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/records` takes `type` (additive): `EXPENSE`, the default, or
    `INCOME`; `SETTLEMENT` there is 400. An income mirrors an expense: `payerMemberId` is who
    received it, the category an INCOME one (422 `CATEGORY` for an EXPENSE one, and the other way
    round for an expense, also on a change), the same split methods, D-12's tie to the receiver.
  - Posting as D-7's income example: the receiver's receipt (`FAMILY_PAYMENT`, link `PAYMENT`) is
    their account or placeholder +T and debt −T; each share (`FAMILY_SHARE`) UNALLOCATED −s with the
    income category and debt +s. The personal cash flow counts the share as the family category's
    income.
  - Edits as for expenses: the receiver with an account changes date, amount, receiver and account,
    also through their receipt with their private note; the author and owners the family fields; for
    a receiver without an account, the author or an owner. Deleting the receipt deletes the income.
    `yourPayment` is the receiver's receipt. The messages say "income" and "received" ("Only Mum,
    who received it, can change the income's date, amount, receiver or receiving account.",
    "Income 5 not found.", "Entry N is what you received for an income of the family budget
    "Home"; change or delete the income there.").
  - A personal entry's `family` gains `recordType` (`EXPENSE`, `INCOME` or `SETTLEMENT`; additive,
    left out without a record).
  - Tests: new `FamilyIncomeApiTests` (4); `DataIsolationApiTests.anIncomesReceiptIsTheReceiversOwn`
    (new; the file has gained 176 lines since `e7cdeb1` and lost none); `FamilyRecordRandomTests`
    with incomes in the mix; `FamilyRecordApiTests` expects `recordType` on a posted entry;
    `FamilyPaymentApiTests` with the payment endpoint's new message.
- The interface (commit "feat(frontend): incomes, settlements and settling up"), ADR 0003 topic I
  ("F4d as built: the interface"):
  - The family pages' "Expenses" tab is "Activity": expenses, incomes and settlements, newest first,
    labelled from the reader's side ("Paid by you", "Salary · Income · Received by Sam", "Settlement ·
    Sam paid you €36.20"), with "Add an expense", "Add an income" and "Record a settlement"; the
    overview shows the latest activity. The paths stay `/family/{id}/expenses…`, which personal
    entries and the journal link to.
  - Balances: who owes whom with the reader's own lines first, and "Settle up" on each line the
    reader may record (they pay or receive it; an owner also between two members without an
    account). It opens `/family/{id}/settle?payer=&payee=&amount=`, the settlement form: who paid,
    who received, amount, date, the reader's account or "Specify later" ("Paid from" or "Received
    into", only when they are a side), comment; a line when the reader may not record it, and one
    saying the other side's part goes to their placeholder.
  - `incomes/new`: the expense form as an income ("Received by", "Received into", INCOME
    categories, the tie to the receiver in the preview), remembering the last account per type.
  - A record's page by type: an income's mirrors an expense's; a settlement's shows its sentence, both
    members and the reader's own side, the recorder's date, amount and comment (with a line that a
    new date or amount moves the other side's part back to their placeholder), and the other side's
    account alone ("Your side of this settlement").
  - The journal's sentences: "Sam recorded the settlement of Sep 14, 2026: Sam paid Anna €36.20.",
    "Anna added the income Salary, Sep 13, 2026: €1,000.00, received by Anna.", "… changed the
    receiver of the income …".
  - The personal editor: a new income has "Family income" (C2), as a new expense has "Family
    expense": budget, income category, split, family comment, the memo as the private note; the
    account list takes only assets and liabilities. Switching between the two tabs keeps the
    budget and drops the category and split.
  - Personal entries: a receipt opens as "Family income received" and a settlement side as "Your
    side of a family settlement", with the reader's own fields (the account; the date and amount for
    a receipt and for a settlement's recorder; a receipt's note; delete for a receipt and for the
    recorder), linked to "the income" or "the settlement". The entry list names "Family income
    received" and "Family income share"; read-only entries name their record's type.
  - The fix after the F4c review: an expense or income stored as equal shares opens its split as
    "Equal shares, as it is split now" (`KEEP`), previewed among its own members as the server
    splits it again, whatever the budget's rule is now, and never sent. F4c's open item about equal
    shares under a custom rule (amounts that didn't add up) is gone with it.
  - Tests: new `FamilyRecords.test.tsx` (10); `family.test.ts` (3 new: settlement and income
    sentences, settle-up order and who may record); `FamilyExpenses.test.tsx`, `FamilyPayer.test.tsx`
    and `Family.test.tsx` with the tab's name, the request's `type`, "Nothing recorded yet.", "Not
    found" and the "Settle up" links.

## 2026-09-30 · Family budget: F4b's deploy and review, the payer's side (F4c)

Commits `3ef8c3c` (documents), `1245fe2` (payment edits and the payer's own view) and `e7cdeb1`
(the payer's entry and family expenses from the personal editor), on `feature/family-budget`,
merged into `main` and deployed on 2026-09-30; the deploy's records: "docs(family-budget): F4c
deploy and review decisions".

**Changes**
- F4b's deploy and the decisions after its review (commit "docs(family-budget): F4b deploy, review
  decisions, F4 stages"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-09-30, `4285d52`, with the owner's
    checks. The F4b entry below got its commit `4285d52` and the deploy's record.
  - ADR 0003, topic I, kept as built: "who owes whom" pairs the largest debtor with the largest
    creditor as a display only, and the fewest transfers (D3 in the review) stay "later"; archived
    accounts are left out of the payment list; money goes through `formatMoney`. Changed: the
    expense page stops finding the paying account among the payer's entries of the day; a field of
    the record's answer carries it (F4c).
  - The stages, in ADR 0003 topic J, `docs/family-budget/requirements.md` and CLAUDE.md: F4c is the
    payer's side (payment edits, moving a payment to an account, the payer's own payment entry,
    marking a personal expense as family, C2); F4d settlements (D2, D-24) and incomes (C5); F4e other
    currencies (C7). F5 to F7 stay. "Later" gains the minimal transfers.
  - CLAUDE.md: production runs `4285d52`.
- Payment edits and the payer's own view (commit "feat(family): payment edits and the payer's own
  view"), D-14 and ADR 0003 topic E, which now says what was built. No migration.
  - `PATCH /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/records/{recordId}?version=` takes the payment fields too:
    `date`, `amount`, `payerMemberId`, and for the payer with an account `paymentAccountId` or
    `paymentLater`. For a record paid by a member with an account only that payer changes them (an
    owner who didn't pay it gets 409, "Only Dad, who paid it, …"); for a payer without an account the
    author or an owner. The family fields stay the author's and the owners'.
  - The payer changes to a member without an account or to the caller, who then names an account or
    "Specify later"; only the caller becomes a payer with an account. The old payer's payment entry
    goes.
  - A new amount, date or payer splits again by the stored split: `EQUAL` among the record's members
    as of the new date (a member with an account comes in or drops out as the date crosses their join
    date; a member added since doesn't come in), `PERCENT` with the same basis points, `ONE_MEMBER` on
    the same member; `AMOUNT` needs the new amounts with a new amount (422 `AMOUNTS_NEEDED`).
    `JOINED_AFTER` and the start date's 409 as for a new record; frozen and stale are 409.
  - The journal records `date`, `amount` and `payer`. The account is never journaled, and changing it
    alone changes neither the record's version nor its editor. Moving a payment between "Specify
    later" and an account keeps the entry; the writer flips the link's `system_owned`.
  - The record's answer gains `canEditPayment` and `yourPayment` (the payer's payment entry, and the
    account's id and name, or `later`), present only for the payer with an account. The expense page
    shows "Paid from" from it, no longer from the payer's entries of the day.
  - `privateNote` on a new record: the payer's note, stored only as their payment entry's memo; the
    writer refuses a memo on anything but the acting payer's own payment.
  - The records' messages say "expense": "Expense 2 has changed since version 0. Reload it and try
    again.", "Expense 2 not found.", "an expense can't be dated before its start date", and so on.
  - Tests: new `FamilyPaymentApiTests` (8) on a fixture moved out of `FamilyRecordApiTests` into
    `FamilyApiTest`; `FamilyRecordApiTests` with the new messages, and its payment-field 409 replaced
    by the start date's 409 for a change; `FamilyRecordRandomTests` with payment edits in the mix
    (from the seed: 103 creates, 59 changes, 35 payment edits, 31 deletes, 12 split rules);
    `CrossLedgerWriterTests` refuses a note on another member's entry; `FamilyExpenses.test.tsx`
    reads the account from `yourPayment` (1 new).
- The payer's entry and family expenses from the personal editor (commit "feat(family): the payer's
  entry and family expenses from the personal editor"), ADR 0003 topic E. No migration.
  - `PATCH /api/entries/{id}/family-payment?version=` (new, only with the switch on): the payer's own
    `FAMILY_PAYMENT` entry's `date`, `amount`, `accountId` or `later`, and `memo`, as the payer's
    change of the expense (`FamilyPaymentEntries`, through `FamilyRecordService.update`); it answers
    with the entry. Anyone else's entry is missing (404); a share or an ordinary entry answers 409, "Entry
    N is not your payment for a family expense, so it doesn't change as one."; a new amount of an
    expense split by amounts is 422 `AMOUNTS_NEEDED`. `PUT` of the payment still answers 409.
  - `DELETE /api/entries/{id}?version=` of the payment deletes the expense with every share, through
    `ledger.FamilyPayments`, which exists only with the switch on; with it off, the 409 as before.
  - The personal editor: a new expense has a "Family expense" switch while the switch is on and the
    user has a family budget. It shows the family budget (a selector only with more than one, D-5), a
    family category, the comment for the family budget, the split (`SplitEditor`, the budget's rule
    by default), and the memo as "Note, only you see it"; payee, refund, the personal category and the
    old "Split with family" go while it is on; "Paid from" offers only assets and liabilities. It
    creates the record through `POST /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/records` with the user as payer,
    the entry's account and `privateNote`: a payment and a share entry, no ordinary expense. For an
    entry in another currency than the budget's it is disabled, with one line that says why.
  - The payer's payment entry (`PaymentEntry.tsx`), with the switch on: date, amount, "Paid from"
    (accounts or "Specify later") and the note, Save sending only what changed; Delete after "Delete
    your payment and the expense “Groceries” of Sep 12, 2026 in the family budget “Home”? The other
    members' shares of it go too." With the switch off it opens read-only as before.
  - The expense page: "Change this expense" takes the date, amount, payer and "Paid from" when
    `canEditPayment`, and the category, split and comment when `canEdit`; a new amount of an expense
    split by amounts needs the new amounts before Save. "To change the date, amount or payer, delete the

  gets `record`, the record as it is now (`date`, `category`, the family category's name, `amount`

  as before; with it off nothing changes:
  - Creating a family budget takes the start date (D-27): today by default, earlier allowed, never
    later, with the line "The first day expenses can have; it is also the day you join the budget."
    It is sent only when earlier than today, so the server's today counts. The server's 422
    `START_DATE` shows at the field. The categories note now says what F4a's merge does: a category
    brought becomes the family's.
  - Tabs: Overview, Expenses, Balances, Journal, Members, Split rule, Categories, Settings (eight,
    wrapping on a phone; none grouped). The overview has the reader's balance in words ("Sam owes
    you €36.20", "You owe Sam …", "You are settled"), the latest five expenses with "Add an
    expense", and the start date.
  - Expenses (`/family/{ledgerId}/expenses`): newest first, 20 a page; date, category, payer and a
    Frozen mark, amount and the reader's share; it fits 375 px.
  - Add an expense (`expenses/new`): date (today, never before the start date), a family expense
    category that isn't archived, the amount in the base currency, who paid (the reader or a member
    without an account), for the reader the paying account (their own assets and liabilities as the
    backend takes them: no system account, none kept per counterparty; archived ones left out) or
    "Specify later", with the line that it keeps the payment under "Payments without a specified
    account"; the last choice is preselected (`localStorage`, per budget). The split: the budget's
    rule, percentages, amounts or one member, with every member's amount, the live total and Save
    disabled until it adds up, computed as `ShareSplit` does; the comment. 400 fields and 422
    `violationDetails` show next to the field or member they name, and a create's 409 (D-27) at
    the date.
  - An expense (`expenses/{recordId}`): every field, the shares with amounts, percentages and who
    changed each last, the payer's own paying account (from their payment entry; only they see
    it), and the expense's journal. With `canEdit`: category, split and comment, only what changed
    sent, with `?version=`; a stale version shows the server's message and reloads the expense.
    With `canDelete`: delete after a confirmation. The line "To change the date, amount or payer,
    delete the expense and enter it again." A deleted or unknown expense shows "Expense not found"
    inside the budget.
  - Balances: each member's balance in words, marking the reader, members without an account and
    former members; "All together €0.00"; who owes whom, the largest debtor paying the largest
    creditor.
  - Journal: newest first, 50 a page, as sentences ("Test User changed the split of Groceries, Sep
    20, 2026: equal shares → percentages; …"), deleted expenses marked, system changes in words
    ("The split rule went back to equal shares when a member left: Former member."), names as the
    server gives them.
  - The personal side: a posted share or payment opens read-only with "Open the expense", a link to
    its page. Screens say "expense" where they said "record".
  - Helpers without floating point: `minorUnits.ts` (BigInt in the currency's minor unit, with
    the backend's `java.util.Currency` digits: EUR 2, JPY 0, KWD 3, XAU 4), `shareSplit.ts` (D-12
    as `ShareSplit`), `expenseForm.ts` (who shares a date, the preview, the request, a stored
    split, where the server's objections go), and in `family.ts` balances in words, `whoOwesWhom`
    and `journalLine`. `AccountSelect` takes extra options.
  - Tests: new `minorUnits.test.ts` (5), `shareSplit.test.ts` (8, ShareSplitTests' cases),
    `FamilyExpenses.test.tsx` (20); `family.test.ts` (7 new); `FamilyRows.test.tsx` (1 new).
    Changed where the interface changed as asked, all with the switch on: `Family.test.tsx` (the
    fixture's `startDate`, the eight tabs, the overview's two loads stubbed) and
    `FamilyRows.test.tsx` (the payment's wording, "expense"). The tests with the switch off are
    unchanged.

- F4a's deploy and the decisions after its review (commit "docs(family-budget): F4a deploy and
  review decisions"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-09-30, `71c3eb7`, with the owner's
    checks. The F4a entry below got its commit `71c3eb7` and the deploy's record.
  - Rounding stays as built: shares cut down to the minor unit, the remainder to the largest share,
    on a tie the payer, then join order. D-12, ADR 0003 topic D and CLAUDE.md's rule 3 say so;
    HALF_UP stays the rule for single amounts, such as conversions.
  - Accepted as built, in ADR 0003: payment entries read-only until F4c, the journal at
    `/journal?recordId=`, the link check left out, comments erased inside
    `release_family_memberships`.
  - D-18: members without an account share records of any date on or after the start date;
    members with an account from their join date.
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Roll an update back": past V7 without a restore only while production
    holds no family record, with the read-only check; once the switch is on (F7), going back past V7
    means restoring a dump.
  - `deploy/pg-backup/finance.conf` counts the family records, shares, links and journal rows
    (`CHECK_FAMILY_*`), and the runbook's block for a changed `finance.conf` names them.
- Family categories in personal ledgers (commit "feat(family): family categories in personal
  ledgers"), D-11 and ADR 0003 topic F, which now says what was built:
  - `GET /api/categories` lists the family categories of the user's ACTIVE memberships too, with
    the additive `familyLedgerId` and `familyLedgerName`, left out for the user's own, so that a
    user without family budgets reads what they read before. A rename or an archive of one through
    `/api/categories` answers 409, "The category GROCERIES belongs to the family budget "Home";
    rename or archive it there."; a new personal category with a family category's code answers 409
    too. To a user who isn't a member, a family category is missing, as before.
  - A personal entry may use a family category. The cash flow, per currency and in the base
    currency, counts it with its family budget, beside a personal category of the same code; its
    totals are the posted shares and whatever the user filed there themselves.
  - Creating a family budget merges: the chosen personal categories become the family's, the
    creator's postings move to them, and the personal rows are deleted. The entries keep their
    versions. Family budgets created before this keep their copies.
  - `/api/reports/integrity` adds a row for each ACTIVE membership whose debt account doesn't show
    the member's family balance: `familyLedgerId`, `familyLedgerName`, `debtBalance`,
    `familyBalance`, with `postingSum` and `balanceSheetGap` 0; the fields are left out otherwise.
  - The demo: it loads when the ledger has nothing of the user's own besides what family budgets
    posted there, and touches no family data. Its categories are always personal: a starter category
    merged into a family budget comes back as a personal one beside the family one of the same code,
    which the list marks, and no demo entry uses a family category.
  - Tests: new `FamilyCategoryApiTests` (6); `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` checks that a user without
    families gets no family field in any personal answer; `FamilyLedgerApiTests`' two tests of F3a's
    copies now check the merge and the code rule. `DataIsolationApiTests`, the one change the task
    allows: in `familyLedgersAreReachedByTheirActiveMembersOnly`, Alice's view after is her view
    before with her GROCERIES and ALICE_HOBBY replaced by A's and B's family categories, and Bob's
    is his view before plus A's family category; new assertions: nothing of B's or of Alice's own
    categories in Bob's view. Carol's view was already checked as unchanged.
- The personal pages with family rows (commit "feat(frontend): personal pages with family rows"):
  - `ledger.ts`: `kindLabel`, now also for `FAMILY_SHARE`, `FAMILY_PAYMENT`, `FAMILY_SETTLEMENT`,
    `FAMILY_OPENING` and `FAMILY_CORRECTION` ("Family share", …), and `categoryLabel`, "Groceries ·
    Home" for a family budget's category, which the category pickers and the entries' descriptions
    use. `api.ts` has the new kinds, `Entry.family`, and the additive family fields of categories,
    cash flow rows and the integrity check.
  - The entry list puts a small line under a family entry's category: "Family share, Home", or
    "Home" for a payment. An entry whose `family.readOnly` is set opens read-only (`ReadOnlyEntry`):
    the kind, a note that it changes in the family budget, with a link to it, the date, category,
    accounts and amount as the list reads them, and Back; no form, no save, no delete.
  - The categories page marks a family budget's category with its name and shows "Change it in the
    family budget" (to its categories page) instead of Rename and Archive.
  - The cash flow (per currency and in the base currency) keys its lines by code and family budget,
    so a family category never merges with the user's own of the same code, and `CashFlowTable`
    marks it with the budget.
  - `Import.tsx`'s kind labels are partial, since the importer writes no family kind.
  - Tests: new `FamilyRows.test.tsx` (9). The existing tests pass unchanged.

## 2026-09-30 · Family budget: F3b's deploy, the start date, family records and posting (F4a, parts 1 to 4)

Commits `c9e58e6` (documents), `71b6785` (local compose and messages), `caba76f` (V7) and
`71c3eb7` (records and posting), on `feature/family-budget`, merged into `main` and deployed on
2026-09-30; the deploy's records: "docs(family-budget): F4a deploy and review decisions".

**Changes**
- F3b's deploy and the owner's decisions (commit "docs(family-budget): F3b deploy, start date, F4
  split"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-09-30, `5a7e490`, with the owner's
    checks. The F3b entry below got its commit `5a7e490`, the deploy's record, and the finding of
    2026-09-29 on the laptop.
  - `docs/family-budget/requirements.md`: new D-27, the start date (chosen at creation, today by
    default, never in the future; also the creator's join date; a record before it answers 409;
    ledgers created before F4a start on their creation date). The planned stages: F4a is the
    backend of family expense records and posting, F4b their interface, F4c what the plan called
    F4b. "Later": the category lists' row buttons.
  - ADR 0003: the status line, the start date in topic D, and the F4 split in topic J.
  - CLAUDE.md: never `git stash`; file copies or a scratch worktree instead.
- Local runs and messages (commit "chore: local compose on a local database; user-facing family
  messages"):
  - The root `docker-compose.yml` runs a `postgres:17-alpine` service with the named volume
    `local-db` (`finance-tracker_local-db`), and the backend connects to it; it reads no
    `SUPABASE_*` variable and never reaches a remote database. `docker-compose.local.yml` publishes
    it on `127.0.0.1:5433` (`LOCAL_DB_PORT`) for the host-networked backend; `LOCAL_DB_PASSWORD` is
    optional. `.env.example`, the README, `docs/auth.md` and CLAUDE.md say so; `./dev.sh` is fine to
    use again.
  - The family endpoints' messages are user-facing: "family budget" (`Family budget 7 not found.`,
    "Only an owner of the family budget …", "The family budget has a member named …"), and shares as
    percentages with two decimals (`Kid has a share of 33.33 % …`, `the shares sum to 90.00 %, not 100.00 %`),
    through `ledger.family.BasisPoints`. Personal messages are unchanged.
  - A 422 of the split rule also carries `violationDetails`, additive: one object per violation with
    `code` (`NO_SHARE`, `NOT_ACTIVE_MEMBER`, `DUPLICATE_SHARE`, `SUM_NOT_WHOLE`,
    `SHARES_UNDER_EQUAL`), `memberId` (or null) and `message`, the same sentence as in
    `violations`. `RuleViolationException.of` builds one; `ApiExceptionHandler` adds the field only
    when there are details.
  - Frontend: `familyMessage` is gone, and the screens show the server's detail as it comes;
    `violationsByMember` sorts `violationDetails` by `memberId`, so the split rule page puts each
    message next to its member. `ApiError` gets `violationDetails`. Tests: `family.test.ts`
    rewritten, `Family.test.tsx` with the new wording and details; `FamilyLedgerApiTests` and
    `LedgerAccessTests` with the new messages, and the details of a 422.
- Migration V7 (commit "feat(db): V7 family records and posting"), additive (D-22), ADR 0003 topics
  C, D, E, F, H and J:
  - `ledger.start_date` (D-27), set exactly for family ledgers: `created_at::date` for the ones
    before V7, today when an insert leaves it out (the image before V7), never changed.
  - `family_record` (type, date on or after the start date, a category of the ledger and of the
    record's type, payer and payee members, original and base amounts, split method, comment, author,
    editor, soft deletion, version) and `family_share` (amount, basis points where entered, editor;
    written only for ACTIVE members). At commit a live expense's or income's shares add up to its
    base amount, and a settlement has none. The base currency can't change once a record exists.
  - `family_entry_link` with its own id, the record, member and link type, `system_owned`,
    `created_at`, `detached_at`, and unique per record, member and type among the links that aren't
    detached. The choice: `entry_id` with `ON DELETE SET NULL`, recorded in topic E, so that a link
    never blocks deleting its entry and stays as the family's history.
  - `family_record_change`, the journal: the record and the author, both NULL for a system change
    (`SPLIT_RULE_RESET`, which names the member in `about_member_id`), the time, the action and the
    changes as a JSON array.
  - `account.family_ledger_id` for a member's `Debt(L)` (a system LIABILITY, one per personal and
    family ledger, `ON DELETE SET NULL`). The debt accounts and "Payments without a specified
    account" are created when first needed; V7 creates none.
  - The posting trigger's exception: a personal posting may use a category of a SHARED ledger in
    which the personal ledger's member is ACTIVE.
  - The guard (topic E), by `app.writer`: only the posting service's writer (`family-posting`)
    writes entries of the `FAMILY_*` kinds, links, debt accounts and postings to them, and it writes
    nothing else, and only to the accounts D-8 lists (a payment's own account only in the ledger
    named by `app.own_ledger`). A system-owned entry changes only through the writer, and is deleted
    only through it or with `delete-all`. A link's `entry_id` becomes NULL whoever deletes its entry.
  - UNALLOCATED can be renamed but not archived (a trigger); no endpoint deletes an account.
  - `release_family_memberships` also erases the member's comments from the records and the journal
    (D-20, topic H), detaches their links, deletes a family ledger's records, shares, links and
    journal with it, journals the fall back to `EQUAL` as `SPLIT_RULE_RESET`, and sets `app.writer`
    to `delete-all` while it runs.
  - Tests: `LedgerSchemaTests` replaces `aFamilyCategoryCannotBeUsedOnAPostingYet` (V6's refusal,
    lifted) by `aFamilyCategoryIsUsedOnlyByItsActiveMembers`, and adds
    `familyRecordsFitTheirLedgerAndTheirShares`, `onlyTheFamilyPostingWritesPostedRows`,
    `unallocatedIsRenamedButNeverArchived` and
    `releasingAMembershipErasesCommentsAndJournalsTheRuleReset`; `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests`
    migrates to V6, adds a family ledger created two days before, and checks that V7 changes no row
    and gives it its creation date as start date.
- Records, posting, balances and journal (commit "feat(family): records, posting, balances and
  journal"), ADR 0003 topics D, E and H, behind the switch:
  - `FamilyRecordController` under `/api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}`: GET and POST `/records`, GET,
    PATCH and DELETE `/records/{recordId}` (PATCH and DELETE with `?version=`), GET `/balances`,
    GET `/journal` (`?recordId=`). ACTIVE members only; everyone else gets the missing ledger's 404.
  - `FamilyRecordService`: family expenses in the base currency. A record has a date on or after
    the start date (409), a family category that isn't archived and is EXPENSE, an amount above 0
    with at most the currency's decimals, a payer (a member with an account names only themselves,
    and gives `paymentAccountId` from their personal ledger or `paymentLater`), a comment and a
    `split`: `RULE` (the ledger's rule), `PERCENT` (basis points summing to 10000), `AMOUNT`
    (summing to the amount) or `ONE_MEMBER`. Shares go to ACTIVE members only, and to a member with
    an account only from their join date. Every other broken rule is a 422 with `violationDetails`
    codes. The author or an owner changes the family fields (category, comment, split); a payment
    field answers 409 until F4c. The payer with an account deletes; a record paid by a member
    without one, its author or an owner; a deleted record stays for the journal. A record that
    involves a LEFT or FORMER member is frozen (409). A stale version is a 409.
  - `ShareSplit`: D-12's split. Each share is cut down to the minor unit and the remainder goes to
    the largest share, on a tie the payer, then join order: 10.01 split 50/50 gives the payer 5.01
    (ADR 0003 topic D now says so; the ADR's HALF_UP would give the payer 5.00).
  - `posting/FamilyPostingService` and the package-private `posting/CrossLedgerWriter`: a share
    entry (`FAMILY_SHARE`: UNALLOCATED +share with the family category, `Debt(L)` −share) for every
    ACTIVE member with an account who joined by the record's date, and the payer's payment
    (`FAMILY_PAYMENT`: the account or "Payments without a specified account" −amount, `Debt(L)`
    +amount). `FAMILY_DEBT_<ledger id>` and `UNSPECIFIED_PAYMENTS` are created on first need. Every
    change re-posts idempotently by record, member and link type. The writer refuses every line D-8
    doesn't allow, and sets `app.writer` for its own statements only.
  - Personal side: `EntryView.family` (additive) names the family budget, the record and the link;
    PUT and DELETE of a posted entry or a payment answer 409 naming the rule; a command posting to a
    debt account answers 422; `EntryKind` has the `FAMILY_*` kinds, which no command makes. UNALLOCATED
    can't be archived (409).
  - Balances: B(m) = shares − paid, summing to zero, with display names and `you`. The journal: the
    creation, each change of family fields with old and new values, the deletion, and V7's
    `SPLIT_RULE_RESET`, newest first, with names resolved when read ("Former member").
  - The F3a checks that were waiting: the base currency is fixed by the first record, a member who
    paid or shares a record can't be removed, and a family category that a record or any posting
    uses can't be deleted (409 each). `FamilyLedgerView` has `startDate`; POST takes `startDate`
    (422 `START_DATE` in the future), which is the creator's join date.
  - "Delete all my data" sets `app.writer` to `delete-all`, deletes the personal ledger's rows
    first, posted entries included, and calls `release_family_memberships` after them; the
    runbook's "Delete a user" follows the same order and says so.
  - Tests: `ShareSplitTests` (7), `FamilyRecordApiTests` (8), `FamilyRecordRandomTests` (240
    operations from the seed 20260930: 123 creates, 68 changes, 38 deletes, 11 split rules),
    `CrossLedgerWriterTests`, the helper `FamilyInvariants`,
    `DataIsolationApiTests.familyRecordsAreReachedByTheirActiveMembersOnly`, two new
    `UserDataApiTests` and records in its scenario, `ArchitectureTests`' rule for the writer, and
    the new endpoints in `ApiTests` and `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` (21 family endpoints; UNALLOCATED
    refused with the switch off). `DemoLedgerTests` compares with the command kinds.

    display name: 400 for a blank one, 409 "The family ledger has a member named X already" on a

- The interface (commit "feat(family): the family budget interface, behind the switch"), ADR 0003
  topic I; no backend change:
  - The switch: `Me` gets `features.familyLedgers`, and `familyLedgersOn(me)` is true only for
    `true`, so a missing field is off. `App.tsx` reads it once. Off, it renders no switcher,
    requests no family budget and registers no family route, so `/family/new` and
    `/family/7` end like any unknown path, redirected to the dashboard; the app has no not-found page
    of its own. On, the rest below.
  - `LedgerSwitcher.tsx`: a native select at the start of the header, "Personal", the family budgets
    of GET `/api/family-ledgers` by name, and "New family budget…", showing the current page's
    choice; it wraps with the header on a narrow screen. `App.tsx` reloads the list after a
    creation, a change, a family 404 and "Delete all my data" (`Settings` gets `onDeleted`).
  - `NewFamily.tsx` (`/family/new`): name; base currency, the personal one by default, with the
    user's currencies suggested; "Your name in this budget" from `/api/me`, with a note that other
    members see it; the personal categories to bring by type, none preselected, "Select all" per
    type, archived ones left out, and the line on what bringing one means; members without an
    account; equal or custom shares with a live total. It calls POST, then each member's POST, then
    PUT `split-rule` if custom (the creator's `memberId` from the POST). If a later call fails, it
    skips the split rule when a member is missing, opens the new budget, and its page lists what

  - `Family.tsx` (`/family/:ledgerId/*`): the name, "Family budget · currency · role", tabs
    Overview, Members, Split rule, Categories and Settings, all with absolute links. A 404 of the

  - ADR 0003 topic I ("Personal" in the switcher, F3b's pages) and CLAUDE.md describe it.

## 2026-09-29 · Family budget: family ledgers in the backend, behind a switch (F3a)

Commits `2c36840` (the owner's decisions), `7db4ff8` (V6) and `9bbf427` (the backend), on
`feature/family-budget`, merged into `main` and deployed on 2026-09-29; the deploy's records:
"docs(family-budget): F3a deploy and review decisions".

**Changes**
- The owner's decisions on the questions of parts 1 and 2 (commit "docs(family-budget): split
  shares in basis points, F3a decisions"):
  - ADR 0003, topic B: the default split rule is `EQUAL` or `CUSTOM`, and a member's custom share
    is `ledger_member.share_bp`, an integer from 0 to 10000, set for every member that is not LEFT
    or FORMER under `CUSTOM` and summing to exactly 10000 there, NULL under `EQUAL`; the API sends
    and accepts integers. Topic D: a record stores its share amounts, and a percentage it keeps is
    in basis points (`family_share.share_bp`). Topic I: the interface shows and accepts
    percentages with two decimals and converts them with integer arithmetic.
  - Topic C: an owner's action by a member answers 409 naming the rule, as the ADR said, not 403,
    which the frontend reads as "no access to Finance Tracker": on `/api/me` it shows that screen
    with only a sign-out button, and any other call throws "Access denied. Please reload the page."
  - Topics E and J: `account.family_ledger_id` and the members' `Debt(L)` accounts move to F4a,
    which also creates them for members who joined before it.
  - `docs/family-budget/requirements.md`, D-12: stored as basis points, shown as percentages with
    two decimals.
  - CLAUDE.md, "How to run tests": `./mvnw -B clean verify` while VS Code is open.
  - The entry below got its commit `74cc0ff`.
- Migration V6 (commit "feat(db): V6 family ledgers"), additive (D-22):
  - `ledger.split_rule` (`EQUAL` or `CUSTOM`, set exactly for SHARED ledgers) and
    `ledger_member.share_bp` (0 to 10000, only for ACTIVE members of a family ledger); a deferred
    trigger, `ledger_check_split_rule`, checks at commit that under `EQUAL` nobody has a share and
    under `CUSTOM` every ACTIVE member has one and they sum to 10000.
  - A family ledger's name and its display names are never blank; `ledger_member_display_name_key`
    makes display names unique per family ledger, case-insensitive, among members that aren't
    FORMER.
  - `category.user_id` is nullable. `fill_and_check_ledger_id` lets into a SHARED ledger only a
    category without `user_id` ("ledger n is a family ledger, which holds only categories without
    user_id") and checks personal rows as before. The posting trigger is unchanged: a family
    category is in another ledger than any entry, so no posting can use one yet (F4a).
  - `release_family_memberships(sub)`, D-20's membership part for the app and the runbook alike:
    one family ledger at a time, under its row's lock, the membership becomes FORMER ("Former
    member", no sub, no share, left today); a family ledger without another ACTIVE member with an
    account is deleted with its categories and members (LEFT members never count); else a custom
    share of the user's above 0 turns the rule into `EQUAL`, and if no owner remains, the ACTIVE
    member with an account who joined earliest becomes one.
  - `LedgerSchemaTests`: four new tests (family categories and what else a family ledger refuses;
    a family category on a posting; display names; the split rule), and family ledgers with a split
    rule and members with distinct names in the existing ones. `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests`
    migrates to V5 as before, then checks that V6 changes none of those rows.
- The family ledger backend (commit "feat(family): family ledgers in the backend, behind a
  switch"):
  - The feature switch (D-25): `app.family.enabled`, env `FAMILY_LEDGERS_ENABLED`, off by default,
    and so off in production (`deploy/app/docker-compose.yml` doesn't set it). The root
    `docker-compose.yml` (local runs) and `IntegrationTest` turn it on. `FamilySwitch` logs "Family
    ledgers (D-25): on" or "off; the family endpoints answer 404" once at startup.
    `FamilyLedgerController` exists only with the switch on, so off, its paths are unknown and
    answer 404 like any other. `/api/me` gets the additive `features.familyLedgers`.
  - `LedgerAccess`: `member(sub, ledgerId)` answers only for family ledgers, never a personal one,
    not even the user's own; `owner(sub, ledgerId)` adds a `ConflictException` for a member who
    isn't an owner; `families(sub)` lists the ACTIVE family memberships. `LedgerScope.rowUserId()`
    is the `user_id` of a new row, null in a family ledger.
  - `ledger/family/FamilyLedgerService` with `FamilyLedgerView` and `FamilyMemberView` (display
    name, role, status, join date, `hasAccount`, share; never a sub or an email):
    - create from the creator's personal scope: SHARED, the creator OWNER and ACTIVE from today
      (share 10000 under `CUSTOM`), and copies of the code, name and type of the personal
      categories they chose; another user's category answers 404 and nothing is written;
    - get and update (name, base currency, validated like the personal one);
    - members without an account: add (joined today, share 0 under `CUSTOM`), rename, remove; a
      member with an account, a FORMER one, or one with a custom share above 0 answers 409;
    - `setSplitRule`: 422 listing every violation (a share for someone who isn't an ACTIVE member
      of the ledger, an ACTIVE member without a share, a sum other than 10000, shares under
      `EQUAL`, one member twice); a share outside 0 to 10000 is a 400.
  - Family categories through `CategoryService`, which writes a family ledger's rows without
    `user_id`, says "The family ledger has a category with the code X already", and gets `delete`
    for an unused family category (the foreign keys refuse a used one: 409). Personal categories
    are still only archived.
  - Endpoints under `/api/family-ledgers`: GET, POST; GET and PATCH `/{ledgerId}`; PUT
    `/{ledgerId}/split-rule`; GET and POST `/{ledgerId}/members`; PATCH and DELETE
    `/{ledgerId}/members/{memberId}`; GET and POST `/{ledgerId}/categories`; PATCH and DELETE
    `/{ledgerId}/categories/{categoryId}`. Not an ACTIVE member: 404 for everything, as for a
    ledger that doesn't exist. Owners only, 409 for a member: PATCH the ledger, the split rule,
    adding, renaming and removing members, renaming, archiving and deleting categories.
  - `UserDataService.deleteAll` calls `release_family_memberships` first. The runbook's "Delete a
    user" runs it as the first statement of its deleting block, and its looking blocks print the
    user's family ledgers.
  - Tests: new `FamilyLedgerApiTests` (7), `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` (2), `FamilySwitchTests` (1);
    `DataIsolationApiTests.familyLedgersAreReachedByTheirActiveMembersOnly` (Alice and Bob in A,
    Alice alone in B, Carol in neither, and Bob's own C); four new `UserDataApiTests` for D-20,
    whose runbook statements include the new function; `LedgerAccessTests` for `member` on a
    personal ledger, `owner` and `families`; `ApiTests` lists the family endpoints for 401 and in
    the OpenAPI description. `LedgerApiTest`'s owned rows include the user's family ledgers'
    members and categories, so `bobsRequest` also proves that Bob changed nothing of Alice's
    families.
  - ADR 0003 (topics B and I: F3a's details and paths), the runbook and CLAUDE.md describe it.

**Verified**
- `change_log.mdx` compiles with `@mdx-js/mdx` 3.1.1, run from a scratch directory.
- After the decisions' commit: documents only.
- After V6's commit, in a scratch worktree of `7db4ff8` (the application code of `74cc0ff` with V6):
  `./mvnw -B clean verify` 261 tests (257 and 4), 0 failures, 0 errors, BUILD SUCCESS.
- After the backend's commit: `./mvnw -B clean verify` 277 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0
  skipped, BUILD SUCCESS; each application context logged the switch once. Frontend: `npm test`
  7 files and 84 tests passed, `npm run build` succeeded; nothing under `frontend/` changed.
- `DataIsolationApiTests.java`: 143 lines added since `ee9e498`, none removed or changed.
- The new isolation checks against temporary mutations, not committed: a member lookup without
  its ledger made Bob's rename of B's member through his own C answer 200 with "Alice's cousin"
  (expected 404, "Member N not found."); a new endpoint `GET /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/probe`
  made `everyEndpointIsCheckedHere` fail with `Expecting empty but was: [..., "GET /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/probe"]`.
- A POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE to an unknown path answers 404 here, like a GET ("No static resource
  …"), which `FamilySwitchOffApiTests` relies on.
- The runbook: all 55 bash blocks pass `bash -n`. The SQL of "Delete a user", as written there, ran
  in a throwaway local `postgres:17-alpine` with V1 to V6: the looking block printed `family
  ledgers 2, owned 2`; the deleting block `family memberships released 2`, its `DELETE` lines and
  `ledgers left 0`; afterwards the family ledger alone with the user was gone with its category,
  and in the other the member who joined earliest was `OWNER`, the user `Former member`, and the
  rule `EQUAL`, since the user's share was 6000.
- Rollback evidence: in a scratch worktree of `ee9e498` with only V6 added, that version's
  `./mvnw -B clean verify`: 255 tests, 248 passed, 1 failure and 6 errors, all in tests that write
  what the application never writes or pin the version: 5 in `LedgerSchemaTests` and 1 in
  `LedgerAccessTests` insert a SHARED ledger with plain SQL and without a split rule
  (`ledger_shared_split_rule_check`), and `LedgerBackfillMigrationTests` expects version 5 as the
  latest. Every API, isolation, delete-all, runbook, importer, report and demo test passed. The
  worktree was removed; nothing was committed from it.
- The deploy on 2026-09-29, from `9bbf427`, as the owner checked it:
  - Before: Flyway at version 5; 2 users, 2 settings, 2 ledgers, 2 members, 20 accounts, 30
    categories, 0 counterparties, 0 entries and 0 import batches; the restore test passed with 14
    tables, migration 5, 2 ledgers and 2 members.
  - The api healthy on the new image; the web container kept running; Flyway applied V6 ("6 -
    family ledgers"); at startup the api logged "Family ledgers (D-25): off; the family endpoints
    answer 404".
  - After: the same counts; no family ledger, family member, family category, split rule or share;
    no row outside its user's personal ledger.
  - A rollback to `ee9e498` ran by mistake at 21:06 UTC. The old image ran on V6 without problems,
    which confirms the rollback path. `9bbf427` was then deployed again: Flyway reported the schema
    up to date, the startup log showed the switch off, and the checks were repeated on it:
    `/api/family-ledgers` answered 404 and `/api/me` held `"features":{"familyLedgers":false}`;
    the owner's dashboard looked as before; the smoke test with the test account passed.

**Open**
- The owner's deploy of F3a with the switch off; the report of this task has the checklist.
  Rollback: the `:previous` image, `ee9e498`, which runs on V6 without a restore.
- F3b: the interface (the ledger switcher and the family pages), which reads
  `features.familyLedgers` from `/api/me`.
- F4a: the check that a member to remove has no shares and a category to delete has no records or
  postings; the base currency locked by the first record; family categories in personal ledgers.
  Once personal postings can use family categories, `release_family_memberships`, which runs
  before the personal ledger goes, can't delete a family ledger whose categories the user's own
  postings still use (the foreign keys refuse it): F4a must delete those entries first or run the
  release after them, and test delete-all with such postings.
- When a member with a custom share becomes LEFT (F6), the rule needs the same decision as for
  FORMER; F3a falls back to `EQUAL` for FORMER.

## 2026-09-29 · Family budget: F2b's deploy, the F3 split, raw ledger ids only through a scope (F3a, parts 1 and 2)

Committed locally on `feature/family-budget` in two commits: `0151b08` (documents) and `74cc0ff`
(the raw ledger id rule). Not pushed, not merged, not deployed.

**Changes**
- F2b's deploy and the decisions after the F2b review (commit "docs(family-budget): F2b deploy and
  F3 split"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-09-29, `ee9e498`, with the owner's
    checks before and after. The F2b entry below got its commit `ee9e498` and the deploy's record,
    the way F2a's was recorded.
  - ADR 0003: status (F2b deployed); topic C's exception rule reads "takes the user id as a
    parameter, or handles no user's data at all", which covers `EcbRateLoader`, and a new rule: a
    repository method that takes a raw ledger id is called only from a default method of the same
    repository that takes a `LedgerScope`; topic J: F3 split into F3a (V6 and the backend) and F3b
    (the interface), and F4a also takes family categories in personal ledgers (the merge by code,
    the posting trigger's exception, personal lists and reports).
  - `docs/family-budget/requirements.md`: the same split in the planned stages, and a new section
    "Later" with the sequential scans on `posting` that F2b's EXPLAIN found in the balance,
    integrity and rates statements, and the two options.
  - CLAUDE.md: production runs `ee9e498`; the importer's dry run for a new sub leaves an empty
    personal ledger behind, which the runbook's "Delete a user" removes, and the importer is
    reworked after F7.

- Raw ledger ids only through a scope (commit "refactor(ledger): call raw ledger id methods only
  through a scope"), ADR 0003 topic C:
  - `AccountRepository`, `LedgerCategoryRepository`, `CounterpartyRepository`,
    `JournalEntryRepository` and `ImportBatchRepository` get default methods that take a
    `LedgerScope`: `find(ledger, id)` on all five; `findAll(ledger)` (accounts by code, categories
    and counterparties by name); `findByCode` and `findAllByCode` on accounts; and `lockAll(ledger,
    ids)`, the FOR SHARE lookups of `EntryService`. The derived queries with a raw ledger id stay,
    called only from those.
  - Every caller moved: `AccountService`, `CategoryService`, `CounterpartyService`,
    `SettingsService`, `EntryService` (its `lookup` takes the scope), `ReportService` and
    `ImportService`, and the tests `EntryServiceTests`, `LedgerRepositoryTests` (by the two users'
    scopes), `ImportServiceTests` and `ReportServiceTests`.
  - `ArchitectureTests.rawLedgerIdsStayInsideTheirRepository`: a method of a `LedgerScopedRepository`
    whose name has `LedgerId` is called or referenced (method references compile to
    `invokedynamic`, so both count) only by a non-abstract method of the same repository that
    takes a `LedgerScope`. The name identifies them: bytecode keeps no parameter names, and a
    Spring Data derived query filters by the ledger exactly when its name has `LedgerId`.
    `everyOtherQueryMethodTakesALedgerScope` keeps that complete: every other abstract method of
    such a repository takes a `LedgerScope`, apart from `save` and `delete`, which take an entity.
  - ADR 0003 (topic C) and CLAUDE.md describe it.

**Verified**
- Baseline, on the laptop in `~/dev/finance-tracker` after `git fetch origin`: `feature/family-budget`
  checked out with a clean tree; it, `main` and `origin/main` all at `ee9e498`.
  - `./mvnw -B verify` first failed once, in `ArchitectureTests.sqlRunsOnlyInMethodsThatTakeALedgerScope`:
    `ImportService$Run.lambda$22(…TransactionStatus) runs SQL but takes no LedgerScope`. The
    classes in `target/classes` came from VS Code's Java extension (the Eclipse compiler, whose
    lambdas are named `lambda$N` and which ArchUnit doesn't attribute to their method), not from
    Maven. `./mvnw -B clean verify`: 255 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped, BUILD SUCCESS.
  - Frontend: `npm ci`, `npm test` 7 files and 84 tests passed, `npm run build` succeeded.
- `change_log.mdx` compiles with `@mdx-js/mdx` 3.1.1, run from a scratch directory.
- The raw ledger id rule against temporary violations, not committed: a service method that calls
  `findByIdAndLedgerId`, one that takes `accounts::findAllByLedgerIdAndIdIn` as a method
  reference, a default method of `AccountRepository` that passes a raw ledger id without a scope,
  and a derived `findAllByName(String)`. Both rules failed and named each one, for example:
  - `…AccountRepository.findByIdAndLedgerId(long, long) takes a raw ledger id, but …AccountService.sneaky(long, long) uses it; call a default method of AccountRepository that takes a LedgerScope instead: Method <…AccountService.sneaky(long, long)> calls method <…AccountRepository.findByIdAndLedgerId(long, long)> in (AccountService.java:87)`;
  - the same with `references method <…AccountRepository.findAllByLedgerIdAndIdIn(long, java.util.Collection)>` for the method reference, and with `…AccountRepository.byRawLedger(long, long) uses it` for the default method;
  - `…AccountRepository.findAllByName(java.lang.String) is a query method with neither a LedgerScope nor "LedgerId" in its name`.

  Without them both pass.
- After the refactor: `./mvnw -B clean verify` 257 tests (255 and 2), 0 failures, 0 errors, 0
  skipped, BUILD SUCCESS. `git diff ee9e498 -- …/DataIsolationApiTests.java` prints nothing.

**Open**
- Build from clean (`./mvnw -B clean verify`) while VS Code is open: its Java extension writes
  Eclipse-compiled classes into `target/classes`, and `ArchitectureTests` then misreads their
  lambdas (`lambda$N`). CI builds from scratch and isn't affected.
- F3a's part 3 (V6 and the family ledger backend) is not started. It stopped, as the task asks, at
  a contradiction between the task and ADR 0003: the task's custom split in basis points summing to
  10000 against the ADR's `ledger_member.share_percent NUMERIC(7, 4)` and `split_rule` EQUAL or
  PERCENT (topic B), which V6 would fix in production. The owner decides, with the other questions
  in this task's report.

## 2026-09-29 · Family budget: F2a's deploy, runbook fixes, scoping by `ledger_id` (F2b)

Commits `69a831c` (documents and runbook), `1af3bb8` (the refactor) and `ee9e498` (the
architecture test), on `feature/family-budget`, merged into `main` and deployed on 2026-09-29; the
deploy's records: "docs(family-budget): F2b deploy and F3 split".

**Changes**
- F2a's deploy and the runbook (commit "docs(family-budget): F2a deploy and runbook fixes"):
  - `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, "Deployed revisions": the row for 2026-09-29, `1f1662f`, with the owner's
    checks. The F2a entry below got its commit `1f1662f` and the deploy's record, the way D3a's
    was recorded.
  - "Deploy a release whose only change is a migration": a new step 1 on the laptop (merge the
    release into `main` with `--ff-only`, push, wait for CI), so the steps are now 1 to 9; the diff
    check says that it prints nothing when step 1 was skipped; the deploy (step 5) writes out the
    blocks of "Update the app" instead of linking there; the restore test's `ledgers` and `members`
    equal the number of users at that moment, not a fixed 1.
  - "How to read this": a block that starts with a `read` prompt is pasted once, and the answer
    typed at the prompt.
  - "Delete a user": `automation-cli` is disabled since the auth server's D4, so the section says
    to enable it for the task and disable it after, or to use the admin console, with its own block
    that asks for the user's id. The first block stops with a `STOP` line when kcadm can't sign in
    or no user has the address; the deleting block and the Keycloak block refuse to run without a
    `sub`. The deleting block also deletes the personal ledger by the member's sub (the member goes
    with it), so a sub without a `users` row leaves nothing behind.
  - `UserDataApiTests` runs the new statements, and a new test deletes a sub that has rows but no
    `users` row.
  - `docs/family-budget/requirements.md`: D-3, the display name in a family ledger is chosen on
    the acceptance screen, prefilled from the account's name; D-19, detached link rows stay as the
    family's history but never block deleting the member's personal entries, one by one or through
    "Delete all my data" (F4a).
  - ADR 0003: status (V5 deployed); topic E's `family_entry_link` with its own id, `entry_id`
    nullable with `ON DELETE SET NULL` (or no foreign key, F4a's choice) and the unique indexes
    only over links that aren't detached; topic G's lookup and acceptance carry the display name;
    topic C's architecture test has a short exception list, each with a reason, taking the user id,
    never reading the security context, and covered by the isolation tests.
  - CLAUDE.md: production runs `1f1662f`, V5 is applied there, and "Delete a user" covers a sub
    without a `users` row.
- Scoping by `ledger_id` (commit "refactor(ledger): scope every query by ledger_id"), ADR 0003
  topics C and J; no migration, no change to any endpoint, body or status, and none under
  `frontend/src`:
  - New `ledger/access/`: `LedgerScope`, a final class whose package-private constructor only
    `LedgerAccess` calls, holding the ledger id, `LedgerType`, member id, `MemberRole` and the
    member's sub; and `LedgerAccess`, the one place that decides access, by an ACTIVE
    `ledger_member` row: `personal(sub)`, `member(sub, ledgerId)` (`NotFoundException("Ledger n not
    found")` for a ledger that doesn't exist and for one the sub isn't an ACTIVE member of alike)
    and `provisionPersonal(sub)` (`personal_ledger_id`, then `personal`).
  - `OwnedRepository` is `LedgerScopedRepository`. `AccountRepository`,
    `LedgerCategoryRepository`, `CounterpartyRepository`, `JournalEntryRepository` and
    `ImportBatchRepository` look up by `ledgerId` (`findByIdAndLedgerId` and so on);
    `JournalEntryRepository.findExternalRefs` takes the `LedgerScope` itself, read in its `@Query`
    by a SpEL expression. The records `Account`, `LedgerCategory`, `Counterparty`, `JournalEntry`
    and `ImportBatch` carry `ledgerId` next to `userId`.
  - Services take the `LedgerScope`: `AccountService`, `CategoryService`, `CounterpartyService`,
    `EntryService`, `ReportService`, `DemoLedgerService`, `ImportService`, `StarterLedger.restore`,
    `SettingsService.update` and `RateService.overview`. Every `JdbcClient` statement on the five
    ledger tables filters by `ledger_id`, and `EntryService` also reads a page's postings through
    their entries' ledger. New rows get `ledger_id` and `user_id` from the scope; V5's fill trigger
    stays as a safety net for older images.
  - What is about the person stays by user id: `UserDataService` (which now also inserts the
    `users` row, so the security package runs no SQL) provisions and deletes all; delete-all
    removes the ledger's rows by `ledger_id` and the personal ledger by the member's sub, also for a
    sub without a `users` row. `SettingsService.get`, the manual rates, and the rate queries, which
    moved from `RateService` into `ExchangeRateRepository` (`findForPeriod`, `findLatest`), stay
    per user; `UserSettingsRepository.insertIfAbsent` replaces `StarterLedger`'s own SQL.
  - Controllers: `security/PersonalLedgerResolver` fills a `LedgerScope` parameter with the user's
    personal ledger after `CurrentUserResolver` has provisioned them. The ledger endpoints take it;
    `/api/me/data`, the manual rates and GET `/api/settings` keep `CurrentUser`. springdoc ignores
    `LedgerScope` as it ignores `CurrentUser`.
  - The importer: `ImportRequest` lost its user id, `ImportService.run` takes the scope, and
    `ImportRunner` resolves `--user-sub` with `LedgerAccess.provisionPersonal`. The demo loader
    locks the settings row as before, and answers 404 if the ledger was deleted meanwhile.
  - Tests whose signatures changed: `EntryServiceTests`, `LedgerRepositoryTests`,
    `ReportServiceTests`, `BaseCurrencyReportTests`, `ImportServiceTests`, `ImportRunnerTests`,
    `StarterLedgerTests` (constructor) and `DemoDataApiTests` (the spied `create`). New
    `ledger/access/LedgerAccessTests`: a user's scope is their personal ledger; another user's
    ledger id and a missing one both answer `NotFoundException`; a family ledger written with plain
    SQL is reached by ACTIVE members only, not after LEFT or FORMER.
  - ADR 0003 (topics C and J) and CLAUDE.md describe it.
- The architecture test (commit "test(architecture): enforce ledger scoping"): ArchUnit 1.5.1 as a
  test dependency, and `ArchitectureTests` on the compiled application classes.
  - `JdbcClient` calls (and `JdbcOperations` or `NamedParameterJdbcOperations` ones) and `@Query`
    appear only in methods that take a `LedgerScope`, except in `PERSON_LEVEL`, each with its
    reason: `LedgerAccess` (decides access itself, before there is a scope), `UserDataService`
    (provisioning and delete-all), `UserSettingsRepository` (settings are the person's),
    `ExchangeRateRepository` (the ECB's shared rates and the person's manual ones) and
    `EcbRateLoader` (the ECB's rates, no user, never for a request). A lambda's calls count as its
    method's.
  - `LedgerScope` is final, its constructor isn't public, and only `LedgerAccess` calls it.
  - Nothing in `ledger..` depends on Spring Security, and nothing in it but `ledger.api` on the
    app's `security` package.
  - ADR 0003 (topic C) and CLAUDE.md name it.
