## 2026-09-28 · One token refresh per session, late login callback (D3a)

Commit `385cd2a`; the deploy's records: `5711645`.

**Changes**
- `SessionAccessTokenFilter`: one request of a session refreshes the access token at a time, which
  fixes the bug D3 found: parallel requests each refreshed an expiring token with the same refresh
  token, Keycloak refused the second with "Maximum allowed refresh token reuse exceeded", and the
  api ended the session.
  - A request whose token expires within the minute takes a `ReentrantLock` kept in the session,
    reads the session's authorized client again under it, and refreshes only if that token is
    still expiring. Otherwise it uses the token that the request before it got.
  - A refused refresh (`invalid_grant`) ends the session only while the refused refresh token is
    still the session's; tokens stored meanwhile by a sign-in in the same session stay. The
    manager's default failure handler, which removed the stored client on `invalid_grant`, is
    replaced by a no-op, so the filter decides alone. The end of a session is logged:
    `Keycloak refused to refresh the session's access token (…), so the session ends`.
  - The lock is in the JVM, which assumes the single api instance; the Javadoc and
    `docs/auth.md` say so.
  - Found on the way: Keycloak 26 (`TokenManager.validateTokenReuseForRefresh`) also detaches the
    client session when a refresh token is reused. The token the first refresh brought would then
    fail at its own refresh, so avoiding the second use matters, not only tolerating its error.
- `SecurityConfig`: a callback that fails with `authorization_request_not_found` in a session that
  already holds tokens goes to `/?login=done`, like a successful login, and logs
  `A late callback of a finished sign-in`. Otherwise the login-failed notice stays. The task said
  to redirect to `/`. `/?login=done` also lands on `/`, and when the browser dropped the first
  callback's response, the frontend reopens the page the user was on.
- Tests:
  - `FakeKeycloak` models Keycloak's client session: one valid refresh token per sign-in, a reuse
    refused with "Maximum allowed refresh token reuse exceeded" that also detaches the client
    session, and refreshes counted as granted and refused (`refreshCount`,
    `refusedRefreshCount`). It answers requests in parallel (virtual threads), can delay a
    subject's refreshes, and can give refreshed access tokens another lifetime than the first.
    It did already refuse a used refresh token, with another message; no test had sent parallel
    requests.
  - `BrowserLoginTests`: `parallelRequestsWithAnExpiredAccessTokenKeepTheSession` and
    `parallelRequestsRefreshTheAccessTokenOnce` (6 requests at once, refreshes delayed 300 ms),
    `aLateSecondCallbackOfAFinishedLoginLandsInTheApp`, and
    `aCallbackWithoutAnAuthorizationRequestFailsInASessionThatIsNotSignedIn`. Sessions are a
    `MockHttpSession` with synchronized attributes, as parallel requests share Tomcat's.
  - `security/SessionAccessTokenFilterTests`, with a stub refresher: a refusal ends the session
    while the refused token is the session's, and keeps it when a sign-in replaced the token.
- Docs: `docs/auth.md` (refresh, the lock, one instance, the late callback, the tests), the
  runbook's troubleshooting rows for the refresh bug (fixed) and `authorization_request_not_found`,
  step 8's note on the registration tab, and CLAUDE.md's project map.
- Deployed to production with the runbook's "Update the app", from `9287f0e` (Change log: D3a's
  commit), after the owner pushed and CI was green. Nothing else on the server changed; the realm
  and the auth repository are untouched (Keycloak was only read with `automation-cli`).
- After the deploy: a row in the runbook's "Deployed revisions". "Update the app" now says that
  `finance-tracker-web` keeps running when the frontend didn't change. The refresh bug's
  troubleshooting row says where refreshes can be counted, since the realm doesn't store
  `REFRESH_TOKEN` events. CLAUDE.md names the running commit.

**Verified**
- Before the fix, with the new `FakeKeycloak` and tests: `parallelRequestsWithAnExpiredAccessTokenKeepTheSession`
  failed with 5 of the 6 requests at 401 and the session invalidated, and
  `parallelRequestsRefreshTheAccessTokenOnce` counted 6 refresh calls instead of 1. The other 7
  tests of the class passed. The late callback test against the old `SecurityConfig` got
  `/?login=failed` after `Login failed: [authorization_request_not_found]`.
- After the fix: `BrowserLoginTests` 11 of 11 and `SessionAccessTokenFilterTests` 2 of 2. The two
  parallel tests passed 5 runs out of 5.
- `./mvnw -B verify`: 235 tests, 0 failures, BUILD SUCCESS. Frontend: `npm ci`, `npm test` (84
  tests) and `npm run build` pass.
- CI for `9287f0e` (run 36426599780): Backend, Frontend and Dependency updates green, read from
  GitHub's API (`gh` isn't installed on the laptop).
- The deploy, on 2026-09-28 from 13:12 UTC:
  - Before: the server ran `070fab2` with all three containers healthy. Backup
    `finance-2026-09-28T1312Z.dump`, `Result=success`, `OK: 4 dumps`.
  - `git pull` went from `070fab2` to `9287f0e`; `deploy/finance.caddy` and `deploy/pg-backup`
    were unchanged. The running images were tagged `:previous`. The build and restart took 33 s:
    `api: healthy`, and all three containers `Up … (healthy)`.
  - The api runs the new image. The web container kept the old one, whose layers, config and
    creation time equal the rebuilt image's; only the ID differs (containerd image store). The
    api's log showed no warning but springdoc's usual one.
  - Step 8's checks from the laptop all as the runbook says, and the auth smoke test
    `11 ok, 0 fail`.
- The production check with the owner: sign-in, the dashboard, idle, then the dashboard, a report
  and a new entry.
  - First attempt: sign-in at 13:16:44, an entry saved at 13:18:05, then 48 minutes without a
    request. The app session and Keycloak's SSO session had both ended after 30 minutes idle, so
    the owner signed in again with a password. No refresh was attempted, and nothing was refused.
    That is the configured idle timeout, not the bug.
  - Second attempt: sign-in at 14:06:49. At 14:13:48, with the access token expired, exactly one
    refresh reached Keycloak (the api's only token call between the sign-in's code exchange and
    the save, answered 200). The entry was saved at 14:14:26.
  - Keycloak logged no `REFRESH_TOKEN_ERROR` since the deploy. The realm doesn't store
    `REFRESH_TOKEN` events, so the refresh was counted in Caddy's access log of the token
    endpoint; D3's incident shows there as 200, 400, 200 at 11:16:51.
  - The api logged no ended session (`Keycloak refused …`), no error and no warning since
    startup.
  - Both test entries (139 and 140, expenses of 2 balanced postings each) were in the database.
    The owner then deleted them in the app: 0 entries and 0 postings remain.
- Not seen in production: the late second callback, which only the test covers. The app's site
  block has no access log, so the dashboard's individual API requests aren't visible; the proof
  of the fix there is the one refresh, without a refusal, for that expiry.

**Open**
- The Excel import (runbook step 10) is still to do.
- The lock assumes one api instance. More instances would need a shared session store and a shared
  lock, or sticky sessions.

## 2026-09-28 · First production deploy (D3)

Commit `5032112`.

**Changes**
- On the server `2.28.108.199`, with the owner: steps 1 to 9 of `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`. Step 10, the
  Excel import, is a separate task.
  - `/opt/finance-tracker`, cloned from GitHub at `070fab2`. `deploy/app/.env` (root, 600) holds
    two database passwords generated on the server and the client secret, which the owner typed in
    at the `read -rs` prompt in their own SSH session. No secret was printed or copied.
  - The images `finance-tracker-api` and `finance-tracker-web`, built on the server one after the
    other. Stack `finance-tracker-prod` started; it created the volume
    `finance-tracker-prod_postgres-data`.
  - `/opt/caddy-sites/finance.caddy`, installed with `caddy-site install` from
    `/root/finance.caddy`.
  - `/etc/pg-backup/finance.conf`, installed from `deploy/pg-backup/finance.conf`, and
    `pg-backup@finance.timer`, enabled.
  - `/opt/auth`, the auth containers and Keycloak's configuration are unchanged. Keycloak was only
    read with `automation-cli`, apart from deleting the test account's user (below).
- `deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, where it didn't match reality:
  - Step 5: a Keycloak 26 client secret has 86 characters, not 32. New: a check of the secret at
    the token endpoint, with a wrong secret as the control. Keycloak 26.7.4 answers both with 401
    `unauthorized_client`; only `error_description` tells them apart.
  - Step 6: the first build took 51 seconds, not 5 to 15 minutes.
  - Step 8: the header checks' `grep` required a colon right after `HTTP`, so the `HTTP/2 200` line
    they promise could never show.
  - `docker compose exec -T` passes its input on to the container. It swallowed the rest of step
    6's block when that was fed to `bash` through SSH. It gets `</dev/null` wherever a command
    follows it (step 6, the end of step 3.3, the restore's `psql` lines), and so does
    `pg-restore-test`, which runs it (the restore's first step, the monthly check). "How to read
    this" explains why.
  - Step 8's browser part: a new account sets its password after the verification link; "Delete all
    my data" leaves the user signed in, and their next request gives them a new starter ledger.
  - Memory budget: the server's measurements, in place of the local runs'.
  - Troubleshooting: Keycloak 26's text for a wrong client secret, and rows for
    `authorization_request_not_found`, `cookie_not_found` and the refresh token bug (Open).
  - New sections: "Deployed revisions", whose first row is `070fab2` (and "Update the app" now adds
    a row), and "Delete a user", for a deletion on request or a test account.
- CLAUDE.md's project map: deployed, and the importer's note no longer waits for a production
  database.

**Verified**
- Before: after the owner pushed, local `main` equaled `origin/main` at `070fab2`, and CI was green
  for it (Backend, Frontend, Dependency updates). `app.finance-nl.com` resolved to `2.28.108.199`
  on the laptop and at 1.1.1.1 and 8.8.8.8, with no AAAA record. The server had 3.7 GiB of RAM with
  2.6 GiB available, 30 GB of free disk, Docker 29.8.1 and Compose v5.5.1. The auth stack was
  healthy, `edge` held only `auth-caddy-1`, and `pg-backup@auth.timer` was enabled and active. A
  safety backup, `auth-2026-09-28T0938Z.dump`, was taken.
- The auth smoke test: 11 ok, 0 fail before the start, after `caddy-site install` and in step 8.
- Step 3, read only: every value in the runbook's table, the role `user` in
  `default-roles-myapps`, the audience mapper, and an example token with `aud` and `azp`
  `finance-tracker` and the role `user`. The authorization endpoint answered 200 for the app's
  redirect URI and 400 for another.
- Step 5: `.env` is `-rw------- root root`, with 48, 48 and 86 characters. The token check gave
  `Client not enabled to retrieve service account` for the secret and `Invalid client or Invalid
  client credentials` for a wrong one. Keycloak logged `CLIENT_LOGIN_ERROR` for both, with
  `invalid_client` and `invalid_client_credentials`.
- Step 6: both images built in 51 seconds. Used memory rose from 1155 to at most 1515 MiB, with no
  swap. All three containers were healthy, the api after about 20 seconds. Flyway applied 4
  migrations (v4), and 220977 ECB rates for 7101 days were loaded. `edge` held `auth-caddy-1`,
  `finance-tracker-api` and `finance-tracker-web`, one address each and none for the database. The
  api reached Keycloak with `200 2.28.108.199`. `finance` is no superuser and owns the database
  `finance`, and no container publishes a port.
- Step 7: `caddy-site install` validated and reloaded, and Caddy got the certificate within seconds
  (TLS-ALPN challenge), with no error lines. The certificate: `CN=app.finance-nl.com`, issuer Let's
  Encrypt `YE2`, valid from 2026-09-28 09:57:08 to 2026-12-27 09:57:07 UTC.
- Step 8: every expected result, with the corrected `grep` (`HTTP/2 200` for `/` and
  `/favicon.ico`). HTTP redirects to HTTPS with 308, and a file in `/assets/` has `immutable`.
- Step 9: both timers were listed. The first dump, `finance-2026-09-28T1057Z.dump`, had 1328414
  bytes and 93 archive entries, and `pg-restore-test finance` printed ok on every line and `PASS`.
  The laptop's pull copied the dump with the same SHA-256 and said `OK: all backups are fresh`.
- End to end, by the owner in a private window:
  - The landing page, the favicon and `/privacy` loaded.
  - A throwaway account registered, verified its address, set its password and landed on the
    empty dashboard. It loaded the demo and looked around: the ID sequences reached 138 entries,
    293 postings and 10 counterparties. It deleted all its data and got the empty state back.
  - Signing out led to the landing page, with a `LOGOUT` event and no Keycloak session left.
  - The owner signed in with Google (`LOGIN` with `identity_provider` `google`) and got an empty
    dashboard. Their ledger holds settings (EUR), 10 accounts, 15 categories and no entries.
- Logs during the test: the api logged only the `authorization_request_not_found` WARN (Open).
  nginx, PostgreSQL and Caddy logged no errors, and nginx served no 5xx. Keycloak logged
  `RESTART_AUTHENTICATION_ERROR` (`already_logged_in`) and `IDENTITY_PROVIDER_LOGIN_ERROR`
  (`cookie_not_found`), both harmless second callbacks, and `REFRESH_TOKEN_ERROR` (Open). A
  master-realm `LOGIN_ERROR` (`user_not_found`) at 11:42, and one at 10:42 before the test, came
  from the owner's own address; `/admin*` and `/realms/master*` are behind the admin allowlist.
- The test account was deleted with the owner's consent. First came a backup. Then its 27 rows in
  the app (settings 1, accounts 10, categories 15, users 1) went in one transaction, with the
  statements of `UserDataService.deleteAll`, and then its Keycloak user, with `automation-cli`.
  Afterwards no row had its `sub`, the realm had one user, and the owner's rows were unchanged. A
  new dump, `finance-2026-09-28T1155Z.dump`, passed the restore test with users 1, ledgers 1,
  accounts 10 and categories 15.
- Memory after the test, from `docker stats`: api 350.7 MiB of 768, postgres 76.7 of 384, web 4.7
  of 64, Keycloak 559.1 of 1280, Keycloak's PostgreSQL 59.0 and Caddy 15.9, 1066 MiB in all.
  `free -h` showed 1.7 GiB used, 2.0 GiB available and 64 KiB of swap.
- The new "Delete a user" blocks, as written, for an address and a `sub` nobody has: the lookup
  printed `user: none`, and the deletion ran its ten `DELETE 0`, then rolled back on a failing
  statement added at the end.
- `change_log.mdx` compiles with `@mdx-js/mdx`. The diff holds no secret, no IP address but the
  server's, and no personal email address.

**Open**
- Bug: parallel requests refresh an expiring access token with the same refresh token. The realm
  accepts each refresh token once (`revokeRefreshToken` true, `refreshTokenMaxReuse` 0), so the
  second refresh fails. `SessionAccessTokenFilter` takes that `invalid_grant` for the end of the
  Keycloak session and invalidates the app's session, and the user goes through Keycloak again.
  This was seen at 11:16:51 UTC, with `Maximum allowed refresh token reuse exceeded`. A save at
  that moment fails, and the form's content is lost. The fix belongs in the app: one refresh per
  session at a time, with the stored client read again inside the lock. `FakeKeycloak` doesn't
  rotate refresh tokens, so no test caught it.
- A late second callback of the same sign-in shows the app's login-failed notice in its tab
  (`authorization_request_not_found`), even though the session is signed in. The app could send it
  to `/` in that case.
- "Delete all my data" leaves the user signed in, and the empty dashboard's next request
  provisions them again, with their email address and name in `users`. The privacy policy says it
  "deletes everything you have in the app immediately", which is true at that moment. A deletion
  on request needs the runbook's "Delete a user".
- `finance-2026-09-28T1154Z.dump`, taken right before the test account's deletion, holds its email
  address and name until it ages out: 14 days on the server, 60 on the laptop, as the policy says.
- For the auth repository (D4): `restore-test.sh`'s `q_prod` runs `docker compose exec -T` without
  `</dev/null`. `deploy/smoke-test.sh`'s comment `Wrong client secret -> 401 invalid_client` is
  out of date for Keycloak 26.7.4, which answers `unauthorized_client` with `Invalid client or
  Invalid client credentials`. Its password-grant check accepts `unauthorized_client`, so it would
  pass with a wrong secret too; the token check proves the secret. No other place in either
  repository expects a 32-character secret.
- Google login leaves test mode once the consent screen names `https://app.finance-nl.com/` and
  `https://app.finance-nl.com/privacy` (D4).
- Step 10, the import of the owner's ledger, is a separate task.
- Bots began to probe the site within minutes of the certificate (certificate transparency):
  `/info.php`, `/graphql` and the like get `index.html`, or 405 for a POST.
- The server's clone stays at `070fab2`. This commit changes only documentation and reaches the
  server with the next update.

## 2026-09-28 · Privacy policy finalized, favicon (D2c)

Commit `88f8cf2`.

**Changes**
- Privacy policy (`frontend/public/privacy.html`): the owner's changes, applied as given; nothing
  else in its wording changed.
  - "What you put into the app": no other user of the app can see your data, and the operator has
    technical access to the database and its backups, used only to run the service, to fix a
    reported problem, or when the law requires it. Other people's names in your data are "not
    visible to other users".
  - The sentence "All app data is stored in the EU." and the first TODO are replaced by a new
    section, "Transfers outside the EU": the app, the login service, their databases and all
    backups are in the EU; Brevo's group companies and sub-processors may access its data from the
    United States and India, under the GDPR's safeguards; Google and GitHub are in the United
    States, only for users who sign in with them; nothing else leaves the EU.
  - "How long it is kept": the web server's line and the second TODO are replaced by one line: the
    logs of the web server and of the login server are rotated by size, so they can be kept for
    several months. That matches the auth stack, whose containers log with `json-file`, 10m × 5,
    as this project's do.
  - New sections "Automated decisions" and "What you need to provide", before "Children".
  - "Last updated" stays 28 September 2026, which is today. No TODO is left, and `privacy.css` no
    longer has its `.todo` styles. The new text uses typographic apostrophes (’), as the rest of the
    page does.
- Favicon, for D2b's open 404: `frontend/public/favicon.svg`, drawn for this project, with three
  rising white bars on the app's accent blue `#0b5cad`. It uses a 32-unit grid with even
  coordinates and presentation attributes only, no `style`. `favicon.ico` (614 bytes) holds the
  same icon as PNGs of 16 and 32 px that Chrome rendered from the SVG. `index.html` and
  `privacy.html` link both, the ICO with `sizes="16x16 32x32"`, so a browser that can use the SVG
  picks it. Vite copies them into `dist/` without a hash.
- `web.conf`: `/favicon.svg` and `/favicon.ico` get `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400` (a day),
  and a missing one gets 404. `nginx.conf` sets no cache headers and is unchanged, and so is
  `deploy/finance.caddy`: the icons go to `finance-tracker-web` like every other path.
- `deploy/RUNBOOK.md` step 8 checks `/favicon.ico` and no longer asks to resolve the policy's
  TODOs. CLAUDE.md's project map.

## 2026-09-28 · Landing page, privacy policy, demo data, delete my data (D2b)

Commit `0cd098a`.

**Changes**
- Demo data: `POST /api/demo-data` (`DemoController`) fills the user's empty ledger in one
  transaction (`ledger/demo/DemoLedgerService`).
  - It locks the user's settings row, then refuses with 409 if the user has any entries or
    counterparties, or accounts or categories whose code isn't one of the starter ledger's.
  - It sets the starter accounts and categories back to the seed (`StarterLedger.restore`: created,
    renamed back, unarchived), adds the accounts `CREDIT_CARD` and `USD_ACCOUNT`, the categories
    `FREELANCE`, `SUBSCRIPTIONS` and `TRAVEL` and ten counterparties, and sets base currency EUR,
    `FAMILY_DEBT` for shared expenses and a share of 0.50. Every entry goes through
    `EntryService.create`, so the ledger's rules and triggers apply as to any other.
  - `DemoLedger` generates it in plain Java, invented from scratch: descriptions as payees
    ("Landlord", "Supermarket"), one friend called Robin, no real people, companies or brands,
    nothing from `data/private/`. 138 entries: 5 opening balances, 12 income, 80 expenses (one of
    them a refund), 12 shared expenses, 23 transfers, 1 loan given, 2 repayments, 2 currency
    exchanges and 1 manual entry (one payment split over two categories). Salary and rent in euros, a
    credit card paid off every month, groceries shared with a partner who pays back their share
    the month after, freelance income in US dollars exchanged into euros twice, a trip paid in
    dollars, and a loan to Robin with 50.00 still open.
  - Dates are relative to the day it is loaded and everything else is fixed. Each monthly entry is
    0 to 27 days before the same day of an earlier month, so every full month has each monthly
    entry once, whatever day it is. The opening balances are on the first day of the first month,
    about six months back, and the last entries are today. At the end: current account 6,054.12,
    savings 9,829.55, cash 253.25, US dollar account US$1,247.00, credit card owing 94.69, and the
    family budget owing the user 88.60.
  - `SharedExpenseCommand.otherShare`: rule 7's rounding in one place, for the generator and the
    command.
- Delete all my data: `DELETE /api/me/data` (`UserDataController`) → 204.
  `UserDataService.deleteAll` deletes, in one transaction and each filtered by the user: settings,
  entries (their postings cascade), import batches, accounts, categories, counterparties, manual
  rates, V1's `transactions` and `categories`, and the `users` row. The ECB's rates stay. After the
  commit, `UserDataDeleted` makes `CurrentUserResolver` forget the user, so their next request
  provisions them again as on a first sign-in, with the starter ledger. The Keycloak account is not
  touched.
- Frontend:
  - `main.tsx`: without a session, `/` shows the landing page and every other path still goes to
    the login. With the backend down, or silent for 8 seconds, `/` shows the landing page too.
    After logout the browser lands on it.
  - `Landing.tsx`: what the app is, how to try it (sign in or register, then "Load demo data"),
    "How it's built", links to the login start `/oauth2/authorization/keycloak`, the GitHub
    repository and `/privacy`, and the portfolio line. Two WebP screenshots of the demo

  - `EmptyLedger.tsx`: a dashboard without entries offers "Load demo data", with one sentence on

  - `Settings.tsx` at `/settings`, in the navigation: "Delete all my data", enabled only once
    `DELETE` is typed exactly. Next to it: the login account lives on auth.finance-nl.com and is
    deleted on request by email, with the address and a link to the privacy policy. Afterwards the
    empty dashboard says the data was deleted.
  - A footer with the privacy policy and the source code on every page of the app.
  - `chunkReload.ts`: the lazily loaded chart goes through `lazyWithReload`. A chunk that fails to
    load reloads the page once, and not again within a minute (`sessionStorage`); without session

- Privacy policy: `frontend/public/privacy.html` with `privacy.css`, static HTML without script,

## 2026-09-27 · Data isolation, auth docs, deployment behind the shared Caddy (D2a)

Commit `84aa3e9`.

**Changes**
- Data isolation audit: every endpoint and service, every repository and native query (reports and
  aggregates included), the importer, and every id taken from a path, a parameter or a body. The
  user comes only from the access token's `sub` (`CurrentUserResolver`); no request could read,
  change or use another user's rows, and another user's object already answered 404. Three
  hardening fixes:
  - Repositories: `AccountRepository`, `LedgerCategoryRepository`, `CounterpartyRepository`,
    `JournalEntryRepository` and `ImportBatchRepository` extended `CrudRepository`, whose
    `findById`, `findAll`, `existsById`, `count` and `deleteById` reach every user's rows. Nothing
    called them; only a convention kept it so. They now extend the new `ledger.OwnedRepository`,
    which offers `save` alone (entries also `delete` of a loaded entry), so an unscoped lookup no
    longer compiles.
  - Reports: the cash flow, per currency and in the base currency, and the balances per
    counterparty joined `category` and `counterparty` by id without a `user_id` filter of their
    own, relying on V2's trigger. A posting changed past the triggers to another user's category
    showed that user's category in the cash flow. Every table with a `user_id` that these queries
    read is now filtered by it.
  - Deployment: the api joins the shared network `edge`, where another project's container named
    `postgres` could have taken the database connection and its password. The database is now
    `finance-tracker-postgres`, a name of this project's prefix.
- New `DataIsolationApiTests`, 7 tests with two users over every endpoint. Alice's ledger has
  accounts, categories and counterparties of her own, an entry of every kind, settings, manual
  rates (one by CSV), a committed import, and a posting changed past the triggers; Bob's has a few
  entries on the same days and in the same currencies.
  - Every read of Bob's (18, lists, reports, aggregates, rates and settings) answers the same
    before and after Alice writes hers, contains none of her names, and differs from her answer.
    Her dollar rates don't convert his dollars, her pound rate doesn't replace his, and his
    integrity check stays empty while hers reports her broken posting.
  - For each of her entries, accounts, categories, counterparties and manual rates, Bob's GET, PUT,
    PATCH and DELETE get 404, word for word the answer for an id that doesn't exist, also with a
    stale version, for her system accounts and with a changed category type. Her ledger is
    unchanged afterwards.
  - Her ids in his writes (14 references across every entry kind, in a new entry and in place of
    one of his) and as his shared account get 422 like missing ids; as filters they find nothing.
    Codes and names are unique per user, so taking hers succeeds.
  - Importing the same workbook as Alice imports every row for Bob; none is skipped as imported
    before.
  - A `userId`, `user_id` or `sub` parameter, an `X-User-Id` header and body fields naming Alice
    change nothing, with `jwt()` and with a signed token.
  - Every API answer carries `Cache-Control: no-store`.
  - The test lists the operations it checks and fails when the OpenAPI description has one more.
- New `ReportServiceTests` test: postings changed past the triggers to name the other user's
  category and counterparty don't bring them into the cash flows or the balances per
  counterparty. `LedgerApiTest` gets the workbook upload helpers of `ImportApiTests`.
- `docs/auth.md`: no more `realm-prod.json`; the production client as created in the auth
  server's stage 5, with full scope and front-channel logout off; production has sign-up with
  email verification, password reset, and Google and GitHub login; `user` is a default role, so
  nobody assigns it; `admin` exists only in the dev realm. New section "One person, several users":
  Google or GitHub with another email address is a separate user with a ledger of its own, unless
  linked in the account console (`https://auth.finance-nl.com/realms/myapps/account/` → Account
  security → Linked accounts). Known limits: `admin-cli` and `security-admin-console` keep full
  scope, `shop-api` exists only in dev.
- Deployment, reworked to the auth server's rules for projects behind its Caddy:
  - `deploy/app/docker-compose.yml`: `postgres` pinned to `postgres:17.11` by digest, on the
    stack's network only; `api` as `finance-tracker-api` on that network and on the external
    `edge`; `web` as `finance-tracker-web` on `edge` only. `api` and `web` run with a read-only
    root, a tmpfs `/tmp`, no capabilities and `no-new-privileges`. Memory limits 384, 768 and 64
    MiB, for this stack's containers only.
  - `frontend/Dockerfile`: target `web` in place of `static`, from
    `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged:1.30.5-alpine-slim` pinned by digest (13.5 MB), uid 101, port
    8080. `frontend/web.conf`: SPA fallback to `index.html` with `no-cache`, `/assets/` cached a
    year as `immutable`, a missing asset is a 404.
  - `deploy/finance.caddy`: the site block for `app.finance-nl.com`, to install with
    `caddy-site install`. `/api`, `/api/*`, `/oauth2/*`, `/login/oauth2/*` and `/logout` go to
    `finance-tracker-api:8080` (uploads up to 20 MiB), everything else to
    `finance-tracker-web:8080`, with the app's HSTS, CSP, `nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy`; `Server`
    and `Via` removed.
    `deploy/check-site.sh` validates it on the laptop with the auth server's Caddyfile and the
    Caddy image it pins.
  - `deploy/pg-backup/finance.conf`: the backup instance `finance` for the auth server's
    `pg-backup` and `pg-restore-test`, with restore checks on the migration, users, ledgers,
    accounts, categories, counterparties, entries, postings, the sum of posted amounts, unbalanced
    entries and manual rates.
  - Removed: `deploy/caddy/` (the block appended to `/opt/auth/Caddyfile`, and a
    `docker-compose.override.yml` for `/opt/auth` with memory limits for Caddy, Keycloak and its
    Postgres), the network `finance-tracker-proxy`, the volume `finance-tracker-www` with the
    frontend target that filled it, and `deploy/backup/` (its own backup script, timer, Storage Box
    copy and restore test).
- `deploy/RUNBOOK.md` rewritten for the deploy in D3. Every block says where it runs and needs no
  editing. Steps: check the server, the DNS A record in Squarespace, Keycloak (3.1 to 3.3 now only
  check the client, the default role and the mapper with kcadm; the old 3.4 and everything about
  `testuser` are gone), the clone in `/opt/finance-tracker`, secrets typed in with `read -rs` and
  written with `printf` (database passwords generated on the server, the client secret from the
  admin console), build and start, `caddy-site install`, checks from outside, backups (conf, timer,
  first run, restore test, laptop pull), and the Excel import. Then updates with `:previous`
  images, rollback, changing the site file, a restore from a dump, regular checks and
  troubleshooting. New tables: routing, and the memory budget of the whole server.
- README, `docs/database-hosting.md`, CLAUDE.md's project map and a comment in `application.yml`
  describe the new setup.

**Verified**
- Backend: `./mvnw -B verify`, what CI runs: 214 tests in 27 classes, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0
  skipped, BUILD SUCCESS. 8 of them are new (7 in `DataIsolationApiTests`, 1 in
  `ReportServiceTests`); `ImportApiTests` passes with its moved helpers.
- The isolation tests catch leaks: with the user filter removed from the rates overview and from
  net worth, 5 of the 7 failed; with the queries restored, all passed. The new
  `ReportServiceTests` test fails with the old report queries (the other user's `SECRET` category
  in the cash flow) and passes with the new ones.
- Frontend: `npm test` 76 of 76 passed in 5 files; `npm run build` succeeded.
- `deploy/check-site.sh`: `Valid configuration` for the auth server's Caddyfile with
  `deploy/finance.caddy`, in `caddy:2.11.4` with the auth stack's digest, and formatted as
  `caddy fmt` formats it. A copy with a misspelt directive was rejected with its file and line.
- End to end on the laptop, then removed completely: a local `edge` network; the production
  compose file with throwaway secrets; the pinned Caddy with `local_certs` and the site file
  imported from a sites directory, as on the server; requests from a curl container on `edge`,
  TLS checked against Caddy's local CA.
  - All three containers healthy; Flyway applied V1 to V4 and the ECB history loaded. `web` runs as
    uid 101 and `api` as `app`, both on a read-only root. On `edge` only Caddy,
    `finance-tracker-api` and `finance-tracker-web`, one address each; `finance-tracker-postgres`
    doesn't resolve from Caddy.
  - Port 80 answered 308 to https. `/` gave `index.html` over HTTP/2 with `no-cache`, HSTS, CSP,
    `nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy`, and without `Server`. A hashed asset came zstd-compressed with
    `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`. Four deep links gave the same `index.html`, and a
    missing asset 404.
  - `/api`, `/api/me`, `/api/openapi` and `/api/reports/balances` gave the backend's 401 with
    `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` and `no-store`. `/actuator/health` gave the app's `index.html`, not
    the backend's status.
  - `/oauth2/authorization/keycloak` gave 302 to
    `https://auth.finance-nl.com/realms/myapps/protocol/openid-connect/auth` with
    `client_id=finance-tracker`,
    `redirect_uri=https://app.finance-nl.com/login/oauth2/code/keycloak`,
    `code_challenge_method=S256` and a challenge, and `Secure` cookies. The backend read the
    discovery document of the production issuer for this, and nothing followed the redirect.
    Keycloak's callback with an unknown state ended at `/?login=failed`, and a POST to `/logout`
    at `/`, both from the backend.
  - Memory after start: api 323 MiB, postgres 64 MiB, web 10 MiB.
- Backups: copies of the auth server's `pg-backup.sh` and `restore-test.sh`, changed only in their
  config directory, ran against that stack with `finance.conf`. The dump passed their archive
  check, and after a small ledger was written through SQL the restore test gave `PASS` with every
  check equal (12 tables, migration 4, 2 postings, 24.6800 posted, 0 unbalanced).
- The runbook's restore, in a throwaway container of the production image with the same logins:
  as `finance`, which is no superuser, `pg_restore --single-transaction` restored a dump of V1 to
  V4 with a ledger, and the balance trigger refused an unbalanced entry afterwards.
- The runbook's kcadm reads of step 3 ran against the dev Keycloak and printed the fields,
  mappers and token claims it describes (the dev realm's own values).

**Open**
- Not deployed: D3 runs the runbook. Only there get checked: the api reaching Keycloak through the
  server's public address, the certificate from Let's Encrypt, and the laptop pull of the new
  directory.
- Caddy's 20 MiB limit was not exercised: an unauthenticated upload gets the backend's 401 before
  its body is read, and there was no real token locally.
- Compose also registers the service names `api` and `web` on `edge`. The auth server's rules
  allow that, since site files use only prefixed names.
- Ids come from sequences shared by all users, so a user can estimate how many rows everyone
  created. No other user's data shows.

Not committed yet.

  `git log -S "change_log" -- CLAUDE.md` finds no commit, and no version of the file mentions the
  log. The rule is only in the README's "Change log" section, there since `3b9b1d8`. Of the ten
  steps of the ledger work, the REST API (`be2f8e7`) and the entry screens (`b63ced0`) added
  entries, and the other eight didn't.

  - ADR 0001 (`1c7b624`), schema and triggers (`5fefb15`), domain layer (`6315cb3`), reports
    (`ccf4bf5`) and the importer (`95ac98e`);
  - the dashboard and the reports in the base currency, both in `2e08797`;
  - deployment (`307e059`).
  - Each one says it was backfilled, lists the tests its commit adds, and says that no test run was
    recorded.
- CLAUDE.md starts with a "Definition of done":
  - every task that changes files ends with an entry here;
  - a prompt's "Done when" adds to that rule and never replaces it;
  - the final report quotes the entry.
- `.claude/hooks/require-changelog.sh`, registered as a Stop hook in the new `.claude/settings.json`,
  keeps Claude from finishing while files in the working tree changed but `change_log.mdx` did not.
  When Claude is already continuing because of the hook (`stop_hook_active`), it lets Claude stop.

**Verified**
- `change_log.mdx` compiles with `@mdx-js/mdx` 3.1.1, run from a scratch directory, since the
  repository has no MDX build. No `{`, `<` or `>` is left outside backticks, and the diff only adds
  lines.
- The hook, run by hand in a throwaway git repository with JSON on stdin:
  - no changes: exit 0;
  - a changed file without `change_log.mdx`: exit 2, and also for an untracked file alone;
  - a changed file together with `change_log.mdx`: exit 0;
  - `"stop_hook_active": true`: exit 0 in each of those cases.

**Open**
- Five earlier commits outside the ledger work have no entry either: `3b9b1d8`, `a4cad13`,
  `11eecf9`, `e313323` and `20829c6` (2026-09-23 to 2026-09-25).
- Three older entries still say "Not committed yet.", although their work has landed: the REST API
  (`be2f8e7`), the entry screens (`b63ced0`), and the login move of 2026-09-22 (`43f6f3b`). They
  weren't edited.
- The hook sees only the working tree.
  - A task that commits its changes without an entry passes it, and an uncommitted entry from an
    earlier task satisfies it.
  - It also stops a session that finds changes made outside it; its message then asks Claude to
    tell the user.
- The hook hasn't run inside a Claude Code session yet. Claude Code reads hooks when a session
  starts.

  secrets, build and start, Caddy, a check from outside, backups, a restore test, and the Excel

## 2026-09-26 · Reports in the base currency and exchange rates

Commit `2e08797`. Backfilled on 2026-09-26 from the commit's diff.

**Changes**
- ADR 0002 (`docs/adr/0002-exchange-rates.md`):
  - Rates come from the ECB, shared by all users, and from each user's own manual rates.
  - Every rate is stored as units of a currency per 1 EUR, and cross rates go through the euro.
  - An amount on day D uses the latest rate on or before D, with no age limit.
  - A figure without a rate is missing, never 0.
  - FX results are computed on each call, never posted.
- `V4__exchange_rate_sources.sql`: `exchange_rate.user_id`, NULL for the ECB's rates and the sub
  for a manual one. The source is ECB or MANUAL, `base_currency` must be EUR, and a unique key on
  currency, day and user replaces the primary key.
- `ledger/rates/`:
  - `EcbClient` reads the ECB's daily XML (without DTDs) and its history ZIP.
  - `EcbRateLoader` stores the rates. It reads the history on the first load and whenever a weekday
    is missing.
  - `EcbSchedule` runs it at startup and at 16:30 and 18:30 Europe/Berlin on weekdays.
    `ECB_RATES_ENABLED=false` turns it off; it is off in tests and in the `import` profile.
  - `RateBook` picks the rate for a day. On the same day, the user's manual rate beats the ECB's.
- Balances, net worth and cash flow take `currency=BASE`. A second handler on the same path returns
  the report in the user's base currency (`Converted*` records).
  - A figure is null, with `missingRates`, if any of its amounts lacks a rate. It is rounded HALF_UP
    to 4 decimals once.
  - Net worth adds `unrealizedRevaluation` and `realizedExchangeResult`, both positive for a gain.
    Cash flow has both per month.
  - Any other value of `currency` is a 400.
- Rates API:
  - `/api/rates`: the latest rate per currency, and the days on which the user's postings lack a
    rate.
  - `/api/rates/manual`: list, add one rate, delete.
  - `/api/rates/manual/csv`: an upload with the columns date,base,quote,rate, saved all or nothing.
    A 422 lists the bad rows.
  - A manual rate needs EUR on one side. One given as EUR per unit is inverted to 8 decimals.
- Frontend:
  - A Rates page (`/rates`): the latest rates, the days without a rate, a form for one rate as
    "1 EUR = …", the CSV upload, and the user's own rates with delete.
  - The dashboard's `currency=base` view shows net worth, balances and cash flow in the base
    currency, with the exchange results as lines after the net.
  - A figure without a rate reads "rate missing", and so does every sum it is part of. The rates
    used are shown with their dates, and rates more than 7 days old are flagged.
  - `formatMoney(..., { rounded: true })` shows converted amounts with the currency's usual
    decimals.

**Verified**
- Nothing recorded in the commit. It adds 28 backend tests:
  - new classes: `RateBookTests` (5), `EcbClientTests` (4), `EcbRateLoaderTests` (5, against a local
    stand-in for the ECB), `BaseCurrencyReportTests` (6) and `RateApiTests` (5);
  - one more in `LedgerSchemaTests` and two in `ReportApiTests`.
- `ApiTests` adds the new endpoints to its 401 list and its OpenAPI paths.
- Frontend: 8 new tests of the base currency in `dashboard.test.ts` (6) and `CashFlowTable.test.tsx`
  (2). `money.test.ts` gains checks of typed rates (8 cases), rate formatting, and rounded converted
  amounts.

**Open**
- The base currency (EUR for every new user) can be changed only through `PUT /api/settings`; no
  screen has it.
- RUB after 2022-03-01 is converted at the ECB's last RUB rate until manual rates exist, as ADR 0002
  says.
- V4 isn't applied anywhere yet.

## 2026-09-26 · Dashboard: period, balances, cash flow table and chart

Commit `2e08797`, together with the reports in the base currency above; this step has no commit of
its own. Backfilled on 2026-09-26 from the commit's diff.

**Changes**
- The period is in the URL: a preset (`period=` this month, last 3 months, this year or last year,
  kept by name so a bookmark stays current) or a custom `from` and `to`. Balances are shown as of
  `asOf`: by default the period's last day, or today while the period runs. A period that ends
  before it starts is an error.
- Widgets, each loaded from `/api/reports/*` and dimmed while it reloads:
  - net worth per currency;
  - the shared budget as a sentence, such as "You owe … to …";
  - income and expenses by month as a bar chart;
  - cash flow by category;
  - balances by account type;
  - open loans per counterparty (`LOANS_ASSET` and `CREDITOR_DEBT`), split by who owes whom.
- Cash flow is a pivot per currency, like the owner's Excel pivot (`CashFlowTable.tsx`).
  - Rows: categories in alphabetical order, with subtotals for income and for expenses, and the net
    last.
  - Columns: the period's months, empty ones included.
  - Amounts in different currencies are never added together.
- `IncomeExpenseChart.tsx` is a Recharts bar chart, loaded lazily, in colour-blind-safe colours.
  `toChartNumber` in `money.ts` is the only conversion of an amount to a JS number, for bar heights;
  tooltips show the decimal strings.
- `dashboard.ts` holds the dashboard's logic without React. New libraries: recharts and react-is.

## 2026-09-25 · REST API for the double-entry ledger

Not committed yet.

**Changes**
- The API serves the ledger of ADR 0001: accounts, categories, counterparties, entries (paginated,
  filtered, with optimistic locking), reports, the Excel import as a multipart upload, and
  settings. The README lists every endpoint; springdoc describes them at `GET /api/openapi`.
- An entry's body is a domain command; its `kind` picks the command record through a Jackson
  mix-in. Amounts are JSON strings both ways.
- Errors are RFC 7807 problem details from one `@RestControllerAdvice`: 400 lists the invalid
  fields, 404 covers other users' objects, 409 a stale version or a conflicting change, 422 the
  ledger rules an entry breaks.
- A user's first request for data gives them settings (EUR) and a generic starter ledger from
  `seed/starter-ledger.json`, once, even for parallel first requests. OPENING_BALANCE and
  FX_EXCHANGE are system accounts, which can't be renamed or archived.
- The user id is the token's `sub`, resolved in `CurrentUserResolver` and passed to services.
  Tokens without a `sub` get 401.
- V1's API (categories, transactions, dashboard) is removed: `/api/categories` now serves the
  ledger's categories. V1's tables stay.

**Verified**

  `jwt()`: 404 across users, reports without other users' data, 422 for an unbalanced manual entry,
  409 for a stale version, pagination and every filter, and the starter ledger seeded once under
  eight parallel first requests.

## 2026-09-25 · Importer for the Excel ledger

Commit `95ac98e`. Backfilled on 2026-09-26 from the commit's diff.

**Changes**
- `ledger/importer/` imports the owner's Excel ledger, exported as CSV, for one user, in this
  order:
  - accounts and categories, created or updated by the code in their `Line` column;
  - one entry per journal row;
  - the optional opening balances (columns line, currency, amount, date), one entry per date
    against `OPENING_BALANCE`.
- `EntryMapper` turns a row into postings, in plain Java:
  - The debit account gets +Sum and the credit account −Sum. The sign of Sum decides the direction.
  - If the two sides' currencies differ, the row becomes an exchange through `FX_EXCHANGE`.
  - The category goes on the `UNALLOCATED` posting, and the borrower (`LOAN_CA`) becomes the
    counterparty on `LOANS_ASSET`.
  - A Family row keeps the workbook's own split: the family's part is minus `FAMILY_EXP` and goes
    to `FAMILY_DEBT`. A warning names rows where the workbook's own part differs by a cent.
  - Entries are written through `EntryService`, so `LedgerValidator` checks them.
- The open points of ADR 0001:
  - FX-gain rows with Sum 0 are skipped. Those with a Sum are imported as income and listed for
    review.
  - A row with shared income (`FAMILY_INC`) is an error.
  - A loan row without a borrower gets the counterparty "Unassigned" and a warning.
- A run is one transaction, with a savepoint per row. A dry run always rolls back, and a commit
  rolls back if any row has an error.
- A row's `external_ref` is `xls:`, the SHA-256 of its business columns, and its occurrence among
  identical rows. A row the user already has is skipped, so running the import again is safe.
- `ImportRunner` is the command line, under the Spring profile `import`, with no web server.
  - `SecurityConfig` and `CurrentUserConverter` now load only in a web application.
  - It writes the `ImportReport` as Markdown next to the transactions file, and exits with 0, 1
    (arguments or files) or 2 (row errors, nothing saved).
  - CLAUDE.md shows the command.
- `EntryService.createImported` also records the import batch and the `external_ref`. New library:
  Commons CSV 1.14.1.
- `src/test/resources/import/` is a synthetic workbook with invented names.

**Verified**
- Nothing recorded in the commit. It adds 31 backend tests:
  - without a database: `ExcelValuesTests` (5), `WorkbookTests` (7) and `EntryMapperTests` (8);
  - against the database: `ImportServiceTests` (8: a dry run and a commit with a bad row save
    nothing, a second run skips what the first imported) and `ImportRunnerTests` (3: the report
    file and the exit codes).

**Open**
- The importer runs only from the command line. The commit message leaves a REST endpoint for
  later.
- V2 and V3 aren't applied to Supabase, so CLAUDE.md points the importer at a throwaway
  PostgreSQL.

## 2026-09-25 · Ledger reports

Commit `ccf4bf5`. Backfilled on 2026-09-26 from the commit's diff.

**Changes**
- `ReportService` (`ledger/report/`) computes every report from postings on each call: one SQL
  statement per report through `JdbcClient`, scoped by the user id. Nothing is stored (rule 13).
  Dates are inclusive.
- Reports:
  - balances per account and currency as of a day. Archived accounts are left out, and an
    account's default currency shows at zero before it has postings;
  - balances per counterparty of an account that requires one (rule 8), without the settled ones;
  - income and expenses per month, category and currency, positive for both; refunds reduce them;
  - net worth per currency: assets minus liabilities, archived accounts included;
  - the shared-account settlement per currency, saying who owes whom. The account is the one the
    user's settings name, or else `FAMILY_DEBT`;
  - an integrity check: the currencies in which the postings don't sum to zero, or in which
    assets − liabilities − equity isn't zero.
- Amounts come back `Money.normalize`d. Also new: `AccountNotFoundException` and
  `AccountRepository.findByUserIdAndCode`.

**Verified**
- Nothing recorded in the commit. It adds `ReportServiceTests`, 15 tests, which writes its ledger
  through `EntryService`. They cover each report; the integrity check on a sound ledger, on postings
  that don't sum to zero, and on a posting on another user's account; and another user seeing only
  their own ledger.

**Open**
- No API serves the reports yet.

## 2026-09-25 · Ledger domain layer and entry templates

Commit `6315cb3`. Backfilled on 2026-09-26 from the commit's diff.

**Changes**
- `ledger/domain/`, plain Java without Spring or a database:
  - One command record per entry kind builds the entry's postings: expense, income, transfer,
    shared expense, loan given, loan repaid, currency exchange, opening balance, and manual (raw
    postings). A command's constructor names every field it can't build with.
  - A shared expense splits by rule 7: `other = round(T × r, 2)` HALF_UP, and `own = T − other`.
    The ratio comes from the command or the user's settings (0.50 without settings), and the
    shared account from the settings or else `FAMILY_DEBT`.
  - A currency exchange has four postings through `FX_EXCHANGE` (rule 9).
  - `LedgerValidator` checks an entry against every rule the V2 triggers enforce, as well as ISO
    4217 codes and category types that fit the entry's kind. It throws one `InvalidEntryException`
    that lists every violation.
- `ledger/`: Spring Data JDBC records and repositories for every ledger table, scoped by user id.
  `JournalEntry` is one aggregate with its postings, ordered by `line_no` and guarded by
  `@Version`. Saving it replaces all its postings.
- `EntryService`: create; update and delete, both given the version the caller read; and get.
  - It takes the user id as a parameter, and validates an entry before writing it.
  - It holds the rows the entry refers to under FOR SHARE locks, and returns `EntryView`.
- `V3__posting_line_no.sql`: `posting.line_no`, unique per entry.
- `JdbcConfiguration` registers converters for JSONB (`ledger.Json`), so the PostgreSQL driver moves
  to compile scope.

**Verified**
- Nothing recorded in the commit. It adds 47 backend tests:
  - without a database: `EntryBuilderTests` (16) and `LedgerValidatorTests` (16);
  - against the database: `EntryServiceTests` (9: postings in order, the split from the settings,
    stale versions, another user's entry) and `LedgerRepositoryTests` (5);
  - one more in `LedgerSchemaTests`, for distinct line numbers.

**Open**
- No API uses this layer; the app still serves V1's single-entry model.
- `EntryService` can't list or search entries yet.

## 2026-09-25 · Ledger schema and integrity triggers

Commit `5fefb15`. Backfilled on 2026-09-26 from the commit's diff.

**Changes**
- `V2__double_entry_ledger.sql` creates the ledger of ADR 0001 next to V1's tables.
  - Tables: `account`, `category`, `counterparty`, `journal_entry`, `posting`, `import_batch`,
    `exchange_rate` (shared by all users) and `user_settings`.
  - Owned rows are keyed by `user_id TEXT`, the Keycloak `sub`, and don't reference `users`.
- Amounts are `NUMERIC(19,4)` and never zero, and a currency is three capital letters.
- Accounts, categories and counterparties have `archived_at`, and postings refer to them with
  `ON DELETE RESTRICT`.
- `journal_entry` has a `version` for optimistic locking, and an `external_ref` for imports, unique
  per user.
- Triggers enforce what constraints can't:
  - At commit (deferred), every entry the transaction touched has at least 2 postings, which sum to
    zero per currency.
  - A posting's entry, account, category and counterparty belong to one user.
  - Only postings to EQUITY accounts have a category.
  - An account with `requires_counterparty` gets a counterparty on every posting.
  - An account can't change its type or `requires_counterparty` so that its postings break these
    rules.
  - `user_id` never changes.
- ADR 0001's status and CLAUDE.md name the new schema.

**Verified**
- Nothing recorded in the commit. It adds `LedgerSchemaTests`, 13 tests with plain JDBC on a freshly
  migrated database.
  - These entries fail at commit: an unbalanced one, one balanced in RUB but not in EUR, one without
    postings, and one that lost a posting. A balanced entry commits, and a moved posting checks both
    entries.
  - These fail: a category on an ASSET posting, a `LOANS_ASSET` posting without a counterparty, a
    reference to another user's row, an account change that would break its postings, and a change
    of owner.

**Open**
- No code uses the schema yet, and V2 isn't applied anywhere.
- V2 creates no rows. Nothing gives a user the system accounts `OPENING_BALANCE` and `FX_EXCHANGE`
  yet.
- V1's tables stay, although ADR 0001 says a new migration can drop them.

## 2026-09-22 · Login moved to the shared auth server (realm `myapps`)

Not committed yet. Auth server repository: branch `finance-tracker-client`, commit `ebe48ee` (not pushed).

**Changes**
- Users sign in through the shared Keycloak, realm `myapps`, as client `finance-tracker`. The
  bundled Keycloak service, `infra/` (realm file and reuse guide) and Google/GitHub sign-in are
  gone, together with their `.env` variables. The details are in `docs/auth.md`.
- The backend is now the browser's backend-for-frontend. It runs the authorization code flow with
  PKCE as a confidential client, and keeps the tokens in its session. The browser gets only an
  `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax` cookie.
- Every API request is authorized by an access token: the session's token, refreshed when it has
  under a minute left, or a bearer token from a service.
  - A token must have a valid signature, `iss`, `exp`/`nbf` (60 s skew) and `aud` containing
    `finance-tracker`, otherwise 401.
  - It must hold the client role `user`, otherwise 403. Realm roles and other clients' roles
    grant nothing.
  - Only members get a `users` row.
- Keycloak is contacted lazily. The app starts while Keycloak is down: the API answers 401, not
  500, and a login lands on `/?login=failed`. Both recover on their own.
- Writes from the browser need the CSRF token (cookie plus `X-XSRF-TOKEN` header). *Log out* ends
  the Keycloak session too, through the end-session endpoint.
- Frontend:
  - keycloak-js removed; the app asks `GET /api/me` who is signed in.
  - Non-members see "no access" with *Sign out*.
  - A 401 right after a login shows a message instead of looping.
- Local development: `docker-compose.local.yml`, enabled through `COMPOSE_FILE` in `.env`, puts
  the backend (8081) and nginx (3000) on the host network, next to the auth server's Keycloak on
  8080. nginx's config became a template for that. Vite serves on 5173.
- Auth server repository:
  - The client, its `user`/`admin` roles and `testuser`'s role in `realm-export.json`.
  - The production client in `realm-prod.json`, replacing the three example clients.
  - In both files, an audience mapper: Keycloak leaves the requesting client out of `aud`.
    Without the mapper, the app's own tokens would fail the `aud` check.

**Verified**
- 31 backend tests against a `FakeKeycloak` (no live Keycloak). Five planted bugs were each caught:
  no `aud` check, realm roles mapped, no PKCE, 500 during an outage, and no refresh margin.
- Against the auth server's dev Keycloak:
  - A `testuser` token got 200; a `shop-api` client-credentials token got 401.
  - A scripted Chrome session worked through nginx (3000) and through Vite (5173): a deep link to
    the login page and back, a write through the UI, a reload, and a logout that also ended the
    Keycloak session. No token was in browser storage.
  - A non-member was shown "no access" and got no `users` row.
  - A session was still open 255 s after login, when the token had under a minute left: the
    backend refreshed it (the Keycloak session's `lastAccess` moved), and calls kept working after
    the first token expired.
  - A backend started with Keycloak unreachable answered 401.
- The live runs found two bugs, both fixed:
  - The loop guard treated an abandoned login as a completed one. It now relies on `/?login=done`.
  - Vite's proxy shorthand rewrote the `Host` header, so the callback URL pointed at the backend.

**Open**
- The production steps in the auth server's admin console, and the production deployment itself.
- Still no Supabase credentials: the stack runs used a stand-in Postgres.

## 2026-09-22 · Integration pass: full walkthrough, restart resilience, reuse guide

Commit `e1cb35d`

Walked the whole loop in a scripted Chrome session against the real stack: Keycloak, the backend,
and the frontend served by nginx. Two parts ran on stand-ins. Postgres was a local stand-in, because
no Supabase credentials are configured. Google and GitHub sign-in used two temporary OpenID Connect
providers going through the same Keycloak broker path: no OAuth apps are registered yet, and a real
sign-in needs a person's own accounts. None of the stand-ins are in the repo.

**Results**
- Signing in through the two providers created two separate Keycloak users and two backend `users`
  rows, owning 5/8 and 2/2 categories and transactions. The second user saw none of the first
  user's data, even with categories of the same name.
- Dashboard totals matched a hand calculation to the cent. First user: balance 5,260.96; this
  month's income 3,650.50 and expenses 1,359.54; by category, Rent 1,200.00, Food 109.55 and
  Transport 49.99. Second user: balance 1,249.75; income 2,000.00 and expenses 750.25; Rent 750.25.
- Killing and restarting the backend mid-session: a message appeared within 0.1 s, the open screen
  recovered by itself 5.8 s after the restart, and the user was never sent back to Keycloak.

**Fixed**
- `frontend/nginx.conf`: nginx looked up `backend` once at startup. After the backend container was
  recreated with a new IP, every API call returned 502 until the frontend container was restarted.
  Right after a kill, requests also hung for up to 60 s. It now looks up `backend` through Docker's
  DNS on each request (`resolver 127.0.0.11`), with a 5 s connect timeout.
- `frontend/src/api.ts`: a 502, 503 or 504 shows "The server is unavailable right now…", and the
  screen's data reloads every 3 s until the backend answers. Writes are never retried automatically.

**Added**
- `infra/README.md`: step-by-step guide to reusing the Keycloak service and realm file in another
  project. A dry run in a scratch project ("acme") followed it literally: a two-line rename, a 42 s
  first start, a matching issuer, a PKCE login through the renamed client, correct provider
  callback URLs, and "Import skipped" on restart.
- Documented Keycloak behaviour, tested with the mock providers. The same email arriving through
  Google and GitHub gets "Account already exists… Add to existing account", so it becomes one user.
  A provider that sends no name gets a first-and-last-name form on first sign-in.

**Open**
- Register the Google and GitHub OAuth apps (`infra/keycloak/README.md`), then sign in with real
  accounts.
- Add the Supabase connection details to `.env`, and run `CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS keycloak;`
  in Supabase.

## 2026-09-22 · Frontend: Keycloak login and the three screens

Commit `8b21e61`

**Changes**
- Login uses keycloak-js 26.2 with Authorization Code + PKCE (S256) on client `finance-frontend`,
  in `login-required` mode. There is no login form of our own: an unauthenticated visit goes to
  Keycloak and comes back to the requested page.
- Tokens are kept in memory only. After a page reload, the Keycloak session restores them without
  asking for the password. keycloak-js only puts short-lived redirect state (state, nonce, PKCE
  verifier) in `localStorage`, and removes it when Keycloak redirects back.
- `frontend/src/api.ts` attaches the Bearer token to every call, and refreshes it first if it
  expires within 30 s. On a 401 it forces a refresh and retries once.
  - If the Keycloak session is gone, keycloak-js sends the browser to the login page.
  - If the backend rejects even a freshly refreshed token, the app shows "The server did not accept
    your session." instead of redirecting, which would loop.
- Screens, using react-router 8:
  - Dashboard: all-time balance, this month's income and expenses, and spending by category.
  - Transactions: add, edit and delete, filtered by date range (default: current month) and category.
  - Categories: add, edit and delete, each marked income or expense. Deleting one that still has
    transactions shows the API's reason.
- The Keycloak URL is baked in at build time from `KEYCLOAK_URL` (the `VITE_KEYCLOAK_URL` build
  argument). The realm and client ID are constants in `frontend/src/auth.ts`.

**Verified** in a 19-check scripted browser run against the real stack, on both the nginx build and
the Vite dev override, stable over repeated runs:
- the PKCE redirect, no tokens in storage, and silent restore on reload
- add, edit and delete on every screen, and both filters
- exact dashboard figures
- 401 → refresh → retry, no redirect loop
- logout, and a session ended on the Keycloak side

A double redirect when the session expires was found and fixed during these runs.

**Notes**
- After a dependency change, start dev mode with `-V` so the container's `node_modules` is refreshed.
- The browser test script is not in the repo.

## 2026-09-22 · Backend: resource server, domain model, REST API

Commit `81f9779`

**Changes**
- Stateless OAuth2 resource server: it checks the token's issuer and signature using the realm's
  JWK Set URI. There are no sessions and no login page. CSRF protection is off, since only bearer
  tokens are used; with it on, a write without a token got 403 instead of 401. Every endpoint
  returns 401 without a valid token.
- The first request from a new token subject creates the `users` row (keycloak_id, email,
  display_name), safely under concurrent first requests. Controllers receive the local user id.
- Flyway migration `V1` creates users, categories and transactions: amounts are `NUMERIC(12,2)` and
  must be above zero. A composite foreign key means a transaction can only reference its own user's
  category. The tables live in their own `app` schema, because Supabase publishes `public` through
  its REST API.
- Spring Data JDBC instead of JPA: entities are records, the SQL is explicit, and there is no
  `ddl-auto`.
- Endpoints:
  - CRUD for `/api/categories` and `/api/transactions`; the transaction list filters on `from` and
    `to` (both inclusive) and `categoryId`.
  - `GET /api/dashboard/summary`, computed with SQL aggregates in a single snapshot. Balance is
    all-time; the other figures cover the requested range.
- Error rules: another user's row returns 404; another user's category returns 400; deleting a
  category that still has transactions returns 409; an amount with more than two decimals returns
  400 and is not rounded.

**Verified**
- 14 integration tests on Postgres 17 (Testcontainers), with real RS256 tokens. The test JVM runs
  at UTC+14 to catch dates shifting.
- Four planted bugs were all caught: a half-open date range, a floating-point sum, zero without two
  decimals, and a missing user filter.
- End to end with a real Keycloak token through nginx.

**Open**
- The transaction list has no pagination.
- Email and display name are captured on first sight only.
- The recommended audience (`aud`) check has not been added.

## 2026-09-22 · Database moved to Supabase Postgres

Commit `16727b7` (committed together with the scaffold)

**Changes**
- Removed the local `postgres` service, its volume and its init script. Keycloak and the backend
  connect to Supabase through the Session Pooler, using `SUPABASE_JDBC_URL`, `SUPABASE_DB_USER` and
  `SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD`.
- Keycloak uses its own schema, `keycloak` (`KC_DB_SCHEMA`). It has to be created once before the
  first start, because Keycloak exits instead of creating it.
- The Keycloak health check's `start_period` is 10 minutes: the first boot against a database 30 ms
  away took 206 s.
- Both connection pools are capped at 5, because they share Supabase's session-mode limit.
- No app migrations needed re-running: the old app database had 0 tables. Keycloak re-applies its
  own schema changes on first start.

**Verified** against a stand-in Postgres, because no Supabase credentials were available: only
three containers start, Keycloak creates its tables in the `keycloak` schema, and the backend
connects. Not yet verified against Supabase itself.
